### AN APOLOGETIC TREATISE

# DEFENDING

### The Integrity of the Society

->OF THE<-

## ROSY CROSS

->*in its defense.*<-

In which it is proved, against the slanders of Mr. Libavius <note>Andreas Libavius (1554–1616) was a German physician and chemist who famously attacked the Rosicrucians as heretics and practitioners of black magic.</note> and others of the same sort <note>original: "ejusdem farinæ," literally "of the same flour" or "of the same ilk."</note>, that the wonderful things offered to us by the Fraternity of the Rosy Cross can be performed without the wicked deception of Magic, or the tricks and illusions of the Devil.

->*By the Author* ROBERT FLUDD <note>original: "R. DE FLVCTIBVS" - Robert Fludd (1574–1637) was an influential English Paracelsian physician and mystic.</note><-
->ENGLISHMAN, DOCTOR OF MEDICINE OF LONDON.<-

<meta>This page begins the formal address to the reader following the title page and decorative frontispiece. ## TO THE READER.

I have prepared for you (kind Reader) a twofold <term>Defense<note>Original: *Apologia*. In this period, an "apology" was a formal legal or philosophical defense of a position rather than an admission of fault.</note></term> in this little work of ours. The first part was published during the spring <term>Book Fair<note>Original: *Nundinis*. Specifically referring to the Frankfurt Book Fair, the primary international hub for the book trade in the 17th century.</note></term> (under the title *A Brief Defense*). Because it served as a precursor and messenger for a fuller Defense intended to be produced and brought to light during these autumn Fairs, I have made it the Introduction to our greater Defense, which is now released. For as to the nature of this **Society of the Rosy Cross**, or who the Brothers of that wonderful Community might be—whether they are perhaps rebels, heretics, or practitioners of forbidden Magic—that earlier Prelude of ours explains clearly enough, using arguments drawn from their own *Confession* <note>Original: *Confessio*. This refers to the *Confessio Fraternitatis* (1615), one of the founding manifestos of the Rosicrucian movement.</note> and from words spoken, as it were, from their own mouths—

<summary>In this preface, the author introduces a two-part defense of the Rosicrucian Order, positioning this "Greater Defense" as a follow-up to a smaller work released during the previous spring book fair. He aims to clear the secret society of charges of heresy, sedition, and black magic by using their own published manifestos.</summary>

<keywords>Rosicrucians, Rosy Cross, Apology, Frankfurt Book Fair, Confessio Fraternitatis, Defense, Alchemy, 17th-century publishing</keywords>

The author is explaining how his previous short work serves as a necessary introduction to this more comprehensive defense of the Rosicrucian Brotherhood.</meta>

## EPISTLE TO THE READER.

...indicated. Indeed, anyone who has diligently studied that work will perceive that this *Summary* <note>Original: *Breviarium*.</note> (to which we here assign the name *Preface* <note>Original: *Proœmij*.</note>) has rightly and with the highest justice defended its integrity against the slanders of malevolent men—who charge the group with the crimes of *Sedition*, *Exorcism* <note>In this context, "exorcism" likely refers to the illicit summoning or commanding of spirits rather than the church-sanctioned rite.</note>, and *Heresy*. It achieves this by using "arrows" drawn from the *Brotherhood's* <note>Original: *Fraternitatis*.</note> own "quiver"—that is, by using the arguments brought forth from their own *Confession*. <note>The *Confessio Fraternitatis* (1615), one of the movement's foundational manifestos.</note>

It was for this reason that I proposed to take up the *Brotherhood's* cause, to immerse myself seriously in it, and to publish this *Defense* <note>Original: *Apologia*.</note>—which is more complete than the previous one—at this current *Book Fair*. <note>Original: *Nundinis*. These fairs, particularly in Frankfurt, were the primary events for the distribution of new intellectual and scientific works in the 17th century.</note> In this work, I have focused myself intensely on clearing the Brothers of the suspicions cast upon them—namely *Necromancy*, *Harmful Magic*, *Impious Cabala*, and *Superstitious Astrology*. I intend to do this partly through divine reasoning and partly through the secret operation of light hidden within the very bowels of Nature. My goal is to wash and wipe away their reputation, which has been spattered with the stains of infamy, as if using the waves of truth itself.

This, then, is the reason for placing this *Preface* before this *Defense*; this is the entire goal of my effort. *Farewell.*

# PREFACE.

*When I had most sharply reflected with myself, day and night, that the calamities of this present life are scarcely ever to be ended except by a happy death, I observed that this human pilgrimage* <term>peregrinatio: a journey through life, often viewed as a spiritual trial</term> *on earth might be compared, not inaptly, to a restless sea tossed by surging waves. This sea brings constant danger to the bodily ship—driven here and there by an uncertain path—and to the sailor, the soul, who is utterly ignorant of the skill* <note>Original: *artificij*. In this context, it refers to the specialized knowledge or "art" required for spiritual navigation.</note> *by which he might auspiciously bring the ship to the unknown harbor of happiness. Whence it happens that out of many thousands of men, scarcely one has directed his course toward that longed-for goal of blessedness* <term>beatitudo: a state of supreme spiritual happiness or "the Good Life"</term>. *Yet, that there is a certain and indubitable seat of human happiness in this world, and that it has been fortunately tracked down by some through long pilgrimage and proper inquiry, is attested by the wisdom of the ancients. By this wisdom, we are taught that* Moses, <note>Original: *Moyfen*.</note> *the high priest of divine* Philosophy <note>Original: *Philosophiæ*. In the 17th century, "Philosophy" encompassed all study of the natural and divine worlds, including what we now call science.</note>, *reached the shore of blessedness; for he held conversation with* God, *and obtained the key to both kinds of knowledge—namely, the supernatural and the natural—through the divine assistance and illumination of the Holy* Spirit. <note>Original: *Spiritus*.</note> *To his virtue there approached—* <note>The text ends mid-word with "accese-"; the catchword "runt" indicates the word is *accesserunt* (they approached/attained).</note>

<summary>The author opens his preface by comparing human life to a perilous sea voyage and argues that while most are lost, true happiness can be found through divine philosophy, as demonstrated by Moses.</summary>

# PREFACE.

...lived <note>original: *vixerunt* (the final syllable of the verb from the previous page)</note> **Bezaleel** <note>The artisan and architect of the Tabernacle mentioned in the Book of Exodus, known for being filled with the Spirit of God in wisdom and understanding.</note>, **Joshua**, **David**, **Solomon**, and all the Prophets: whose wisdom was also imitated by several of the ancient Philosophers, among whom **Hermes** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> **Trismegistus** <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure, "Thrice-Greatest Hermes," to whom various mystical, alchemical, and philosophical writings are attributed.</note> seems to take and claim the first place for himself. His *Sacred Discourses* <note>original: *Sermones ejus sacri* (referring to the *Hermetica*)</note> and his knowledge in the *Emerald Tablets* <note>original: *tabulis Smaragdinīs*; a foundational text of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note> have vividly depicted for us his wondrous knowledge of things above and things below. Nor certainly can the praises of **Apollonius the Pythagorean**, so celebrated by **Philostratos**, be covered or overwhelmed by the forgetfulness or silence of men; by his authority it was made known that he understood all secrets, recalled the dead to life, cured diseases by touch alone, excelled <note>original: *polluisse* (likely a typo for *pollere*, to be powerful/strong) in holiness after his own fashion,</note> and abounded in every happiness of this world. From him we also learn that a brotherhood of Wise Men flourished at that time in **India**, to whom it was granted to predict future events for men, and to read and divine their conditions, parents, fortunes, and names, as if they were carved into their faces by the secret characters of nature. The wisdom of the **Egyptians** and **Ethiopians** must also be committed to eternal monuments of memory, from whose fountains and springs **Plato** is said to have drawn the Idea of his divine knowledge. All these men, without doubt (if they were free from diabolical superstitions), reached the highest and most royal peak of happiness and worldly wisdom. Therefore, thinking upon these things with concern again and again, I perceived that nothing in this world is more eagerly to be sought by men than happiness, and nothing...

...too little. <note>original: *parum* (completing the word from the previous page fragment "pa-")</note> More earnestly must true wisdom be investigated, because it has revealed to men a long life secure from all poverty, a contempt for the world, divine contemplation, a knowledge of mysteries both natural and supernatural, and a pure revelation of divine majesty, all to be received with wonder. 

But truly, in reflecting on this, I am distressed with no small grief that the ancient wise men sowed the tree and the fruit of their knowledge so sparingly and greedily <note>The author critiquing the "hermetic" or secretive nature of ancient philosophers who did not share their findings.</note> that they have benefited our own age not at all. For where in the world (alas!) and in what corner of the globe should we search for the fountain and origin of that ancient wisdom? If we flee for the sake of counsel to the **Sacred Bible**, in which the most precious treasure of all is stored, we will surely find there letters that kill and lead into error, so that without the spirit of the living God—whose role alone is to give life—we cannot draw divine mysteries from it. As seen in **2 Corinthians 3** <note>Refers to 2 Corinthians 3:6: "the letter kills, but the Spirit gives life."</note>, for under the husk <note>original: *cortice* (literally "bark" or "cortex"); a common metaphor for the literal meaning of scripture hiding a spiritual core.</note> of words, divine secrets lie hidden and concealed. 

But since the Lord God is gracious and full of mercy, he has promised that he would open the door of his knowledge and revelation to us if we knock. Therefore, let us seek; let us **not quench the spirit nor despise prophecies**, but **test all things and hold fast to what is good** (**1 Thessalonians 5**).

# PREFACE.

...in proclaiming <note>original: *ebuccinando* (literally "to trumpet forth")</note> mysteries both divine and natural. Why, therefore, should the **community** <note>original: *cœnobium*; a term usually referring to a monastery or a communal religious living space.</note> of this order not be diligently sought after? And once found through such inquiry, why should it not finally be approached? For it has been spread abroad in both writings and speeches that this brotherhood will offer itself to us freely, of its own accord, without any force applied, and without cost.

Or why should such gifts of wisdom offered to us be so lightly rejected, when—as **Hippocrates** <note>Hippocrates of Kos (c. 460 – c. 370 BC), the ancient Greek physician often called the "Father of Medicine."</note> testifies—*life is short, but art is long, and opportunity is fleeting*? <note>original: *vita sit brevis, ars verò longa, & occasio volucris*. This is the famous opening aphorism of Hippocrates.</note> To this doubt, **Dr. Libavius** <note>Andreas Libavius (1555–1616), a German physician and chemist. Though a believer in alchemy, he was a fierce critic of the Rosicrucians, whom he viewed as disruptive and heretical.</note> certainly seems to respond in his *Analysis of the Confession of the Rosy Cross Fraternity*. However, in that work—if we weigh his writings with a careful eye—we will discover more bitterness and "pen-malevolence" than any fundamental investigation of the matter. 

For in one place, he brings the brothers under suspicion of **sedition** <note>meaning inciting rebellion against the state or church.</note>. Elsewhere, he contends that their promises cannot be fulfilled or performed without detestable **magic** or **diabolical illusions**. Then, he strives to stain them with **heresy**, perhaps contaminating the truth with a lie. It is usually held to be an extreme injustice to accuse another or condemn them for a crime or wickedness based merely on "effects," as it were, before the cause is accurately explored and examined in the proper way.

Hence it is that I have proposed in my mind to defend the cause of the fraternity against these slanderous aspersions of Dr. Libavius and others, using both their own weapons and those taken from the quivers of others. Let us hear, I pray, the brothers speaking for themselves in their *Confession*...

*...by which (as I believe) they have excused themselves to the world in those very matters for which Dr. Libavius accused them, and in this way they have sufficiently satisfied the wise.* <note>The author is arguing that the Rosicrucians' own "Confessio" (Confession) serves as a better legal defense than Libavius's attacks.</note> *For they confess that it is the plan of God to exalt the humble, but to crush the proud with contempt; thus every occasion for **sedition** <note>original: *seditionis*; inciting rebellion against authority.</note> (if they speak openly and readily) must be taken away from them, since the mother of sedition is ambition, which pride is accustomed to feed and nourish. They also confess that they embrace a divine life, taking delight in the **contemplation** of God and all of naked Nature, and holding the world in contempt, since it is a valley of miseries, in which no fortune is lasting, and there is no happiness before the end. By this confession indeed (unless I am mistaken) they have cleared themselves of this crime and removed every suspicion <note>original: *imaginationem* (literally "imagining")</note> of sedition. Elsewhere in the confession we also find that they embrace Christ purely and sincerely and lead a Christian life; for which reason all suspicion of **Diabolical art** <note>meaning black magic or witchcraft, a serious legal charge in the 17th century.</note> must be removed far from them, and banished by men to the farthest shores of oblivion. For those who truly and sincerely worship Christ are able to accomplish greater things through His most holy virtue and lead them to a happy conclusion, than through the deceptive and vain illusions of the **Devil**—from which we conclude these brothers are entirely free; since in one part of their confession they declare themselves to be devoted to true philosophy and dedicated to **Gymnosophistic** <term>Gymnosophistic: referring to the "naked philosophers" of ancient India, whom early modern thinkers admired for their asceticism and direct study of nature.</term> wisdom; and in another, that this movement of theirs did not proceed im-*

...[not] by the impulse of their own free will, but by the inspiration <note>original: *ambitione*; here used in the sense of the Holy Spirit's pervasive influence or "going about" its work.</note> of the Holy Spirit, whose nature it is to teach all things not through deceits, but with pure and most essential truth. From these things we understand that this society observes not only the mere laws of Nature and her naked processes in the secrets they propose to us, but also that to execute them justly and lead them to the determined goal by a straight path, they seek not the aid of a malignant spirit, but of the divine and sacrosanct Spirit. Without His divine will, the true key of knowledge is not to be obtained. 

For what, I ask, would it profit the brothers to announce their wonderful works to the world with haughty speech and a blast as if from a trumpet, and to soothe the ears of men with empty rumors, if the promise of their word could not be fulfilled without the wicked tricks of **Magic**? For in every **Christian Commonwealth**, it is decreed and ordained by specific law that **Necromancers**, **Sorcerers** <note>original: *Venefici*; literally "poisoners," but in a 17th-century legal context, it referred to those who practiced harmful magic or witchcraft.</note>, and **Enchanters** should be punished with the death penalty, whether by the gallows or by fire. 

But since **Dr. Libavius** <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1550–1616), a German physician and chemist who famously attacked the Rosicrucian manifestos, accusing them of being both scientifically unsound and heretical.</note> seems so fixed in that opinion and view that he can in no way be moved from it—namely, that such works, so marvelous to the world, cannot be accomplished and performed without the Devil's help—we have published this **Apology** <note>An *Apologia* is a formal written defense of a doctrine or person.</note> of ours. In it, we shall declare it probable that the intentions of the brothers made known to the public shine forth and are brought into view partly by divine gift, and partly by secret reasons and the light enclosed and kept hidden within the very bowels of Nature. But now, since all the hiding places of suspicion have been traversed by Dr. Libavius, into that...

...[he] even finally noticed that the brothers had fallen into **heresy**, on the grounds that they promised to reform the defects of the arts and to heal and correct their ailments; And yet we see that in **Theology**—the most excellent of sciences—the context of the **Holy Bible** is constantly called into controversy, since one school of theologians interprets it in one sense, and another in another. Each school is accustomed to explain and twist any of the more difficult passages of the scriptures in its own way, and this is done without any suspicion of heresy—although it is the habit and custom of the Romans and **Papists** <note>A 17th-century term for followers of the Roman Catholic Church, often used by Protestant writers to imply political and religious subjection to the Pope.</note> to most gravely accuse those of heresy who do not constantly adhere to their religion. 

Thus **Lutherans**, **Calvinists**, and others of this kind—and likewise these brothers—are to be held as heretics because they have condemned the Pope along with **Muhammad** <note>original: *Mahumeta*. The author is grouping the Papacy with Islam as external threats to "true" Christian spirituality, a common rhetorical move in Reformation-era polemics.</note>, if any faith is to be placed in the vain and threatening assertions of the Papists. 

Having therefore carefully weighed these things in my mind, I perceived it to be a negligence in man—one that can never be punished enough by the whip of ignorance—to lightly refuse and reject such gifts of wisdom, which are perhaps offered to us by divine power. For it is the part of a wise man to accept what is good with a grateful heart, and to sagaciously reject what is evil as unworthy. For "prophecies are not to be despised, but all are to be tested and those which are good are to be retained" (*1 Thessalonians 5*). We are also exhorted to follow love and to desire **spiritual gifts** so that we may prophesy from them (*1 Corinthians 14*). I say, therefore, that the propositions of the brothers are not to be despised, since it is possible that the true [inspiration] of the **Holy Spirit**...

# PREFACE

enlightenment and a full abundance of knowledge may be appropriately distributed by God even among individual men of this age, according to that word of the prophet Joel, chapter 2: God promised through the mouths of his Prophets that he wishes to pour out his spirit upon all flesh, and the sons and the daughters of men shall prophesy, and the young men shall see visions, and the old men shall have dreams. Similarly, he promised he would give to all those who would call upon him in that name the Holy Spirit, who would teach them all truth. *Luke 12, John 14, 15.* *And elsewhere, They shall all be taught by God. Isaiah 54, Jeremiah 31, John 6.* From him alone, and from no other, true wisdom and the key of knowledge is to be obtained.

# PREFACE

...whether these things are found in the brotherhood or not. It is written that the gifts of the Holy Spirit shine forth in each individual for the common benefit (*1 Corinthians 12*). <note>The author cites 1 Corinthians 12:7: "To each is given the manifestation of the Spirit for the common good."</note> Thus **Moses**, **Aaron**, and other **Patriarchs** and **Prophets** did not receive the Holy Spirit for their own sake, but so that they might teach, encourage, admonish, rebuke, console, and govern the people of God in the fear of the Lord; therefore, they publicly displayed their spiritual and worldly <note>original: *secularia*. In this context, it refers to practical or earthly knowledge as opposed to purely divine revelation.</note> gifts. It is, therefore, no small sign of the enjoyment of the Holy Spirit in the brothers that they have also set forth their prophecies and sciences in public for the common benefit. 

But we must still inquire further and contemplate more deeply whether their actions and deeds agree with the other gifts of the spirit. First, let us reflect with a willing mind on what signs and testimonies prove the presence of the Holy Spirit in men; then, let us follow and trace their footsteps in their *Confession* <note>original: *confessione*. This is likely a reference to the *Confessio Fraternitatis* (1615), one of the founding manifestos of the Rosicrucian movement.</note> and look deeply within to see how many impressions of the aforementioned testimonies are discovered there. Therefore, whoever speaks the truth, prophesies, sees true visions, dreams dreams, speaks in new tongues, interprets Scripture, casts out demons, perfectly cures and heals the sick, observes divine precepts, does not resist the Word of God, and produces the fruits of the Spirit—such as **Love**, **Joy**, **Peace**, **Charity**, **Generosity**, **Kindness**, **Goodness**, **Gentleness**, **Temperance**, and **Chastity** <note>These "fruits of the Spirit" are drawn from Galatians 5:22-23.</note>—and does not perform the works of the flesh, such as **Fornication**,

Impurity, Impudence, Idolatry, Sorcery, Enmity, Murder, Gluttony, Anger, Discord, and other things of that kind—these, without a doubt, are from God and full of His most holy Spirit! <note>The author is using heavy irony here. While it translates to "medications," in this 17th-century context, it refers to the entire practice of medical treatment and healing.</note> for free.

...may shine forth for the brothers of this community (if their deeds harmonize with their words in the same tone). This, therefore, is the chief reason why I have committed myself to their cause: so that we might remove the perhaps ill-founded suspicions of Lord Libavius <note>Andreas Libavius (1555–1616) was a famous German physician and chemist who published several attacks against the Rosicrucian manifestos, accusing them of being magicians or subversives.</note> and others. For, by Hercules <note>original: *herculè*. A common Latin exclamation used to emphasize a point, similar to saying "by God" or "indeed."</note>, such men are skilled at using the most perverse opinions to judge as either diabolical or lies those things which seem new to the ear, crude and foreign to the sight, or certainly difficult beyond the grasp of the imagination. 

Yet, if they explored these things a little more accurately, they would perceive them to be not only evident once discovered, but even easier to perform without any demonic art. We shall declare this clearly in this, our *Apology* <note>original: *Apologia*. A formal written defense of a position or group.</note>, in which we have proposed two paths—the truest and most certain of all—by which prophecy <term>vaticinium (the power of foretelling the future)</term>, the knowledge of spiritual and worldly secrets <term>arcana (hidden mysteries or "inner workings" of the world)</term>, and the execution of things commonly admired may be fully completed and led to a desired end. 

The first path is by the outpouring of the Holy Spirit <note>original: *Spiritus S.* (Spiritus Sancti)</note> into men, in the way we read that the Apostles performed their miracles. The second path is by the revelation of mysteries enclosed in the hidden bowels of Nature, which also requires the assistance of a spirit; and in this way, Solomon <note>King Solomon was traditionally viewed by Renaissance scholars and alchemists as the ultimate master of "natural magic"—the ability to understand and use the hidden properties of plants, stones, and animals.</note> was well-versed in every kind of wisdom. 

It also happens sometimes that the Devil, through his impious seers—namely, the Necromancers <note>Those who practice divination by communicating with the dead.</note>—speaks the truth and gives forth divinations of future things. Yet he could not do this without the divine will, for without God's permission, his prophecies are only conjectures interspersed with lies. *Impurity, Impudence, Idolatry, Sorcery <note>original: veneficium. # PREFACE

...[may] shine forth for the brothers of this monastery (if their deeds harmonize with their words in the same key <note>original: *tono*. The author uses a musical metaphor to describe the necessary alignment between a person's actions and their claims of holiness.</note>). This, therefore, is the chief reason why I have cast myself into their cause: so that we might remove the perhaps ill-founded suspicions of Master Libavius <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1555–1616) was a prominent German physician and chemist. While he was a pioneer in chemistry, he was a fierce critic of the Rosicrucians, whom he suspected of practicing black magic and subverting social order.</note> and others. These men, by Hercules <note>original: *hercule*. A common Latin exclamation used to emphasize the truth or intensity of a statement.</note>, are skilled at using the most wicked opinions to judge things as either Diabolical or lies—things which seem new to the ear, or crude and foreign to the eye, or certainly difficult and beyond the grasp of the imagination. Yet, if they were to explore these matters a little more accurately, they would find them not only evident upon discovery, but even easier to perform without any demonic art.

We shall clearly declare this in this Apology <term>Apologia (a formal defense of a set of beliefs or actions, often written to answer specific accusations)</term> of ours, in which we have proposed two ways, the truest and most certain of all, by which prophecy <term>vaticinium (the power of foretelling the future or speaking via divine inspiration)</term>, the knowledge of spiritual and secular secrets, and the performance of things commonly wondered at may be fully accomplished and led to the desired end. These are: namely, either by the pouring out of the Holy Spirit into men—the way we read that the Apostles performed their miracles—or by the revelation of mysteries locked away in the hidden bowels <note>original: *visceribus*. This term refers to the innermost, deepest parts of the natural world where God has "hidden" the secrets of alchemy and physics.</note> of Nature, which also requires the assistance of the spirit; and in this way Solomon was practiced in every kind of wisdom.

It also happens sometimes that the Devil, through his impious prophets—namely, Necromancers <term>Necromanticos (practitioners of 'black' magic who were believed to consult the spirits of the dead)</term>—speaks the truth and utters divinations of the future. Yet he could not do this without the divine will, without which his prophecies are merely conjectures interspersed with lies. Hence, the spirit of the Devil, poured out into the world by his own judgment, is either a liar, or equivocal, or...

<meta>catchword: enigmatic- (ænigma-)</meta>

<summary>The author defends the Rosicrucian "wonders" against critics like Libavius, arguing they stem from the Holy Spirit or natural philosophy rather than sorcery. He distinguishes between divine wisdom, like that of Solomon, and the deceptive, permitted divinations of the Devil.</summary>

-># PREFACE<-

...enigmatic. In this way, that diabolical apparition which appeared to Brutus was a liar, inciting him to battle with false persuasions and promising victory; by obeying its warnings, he was ultimately killed in the line of battle <note>original: *acies*. This refers to the "evil spirit" that allegedly appeared to Marcus Junius Brutus before the Battle of Philippi in 42 BCE, famously dramatized by Plutarch and later Shakespeare.</note>. In the same way, that uncertain and enigmatic spirit of Apollo directed that oracle of the Pagans and governed the tongue of the frenzied Pythia <note>The Pythia was the high priestess of the Temple of Apollo at Delphi, who delivered oracles in a state of trance or "frenzy" induced by vapors from the earth.</note>. Sometimes this malignant spirit will mix truth with falsehood—specifically by occasionally announcing the truth to men so that he may <unclear>afterwards</unclear> more easily deceive those who entrust their faith to him with his absurd and monstrous lies, leading them to inevitable ruin. By this means, he also very often plots the sudden destruction of men, which he brings to effect either in his own person—through thunder, lightning, earthquakes, the disturbance of waters, the overturning of buildings, the stirring up of violent winds, and similar things—or accomplishes through his ministers, such as **Poisoners** <term>Venefici (historically, those who used drugs, potions, or poisons for harmful magic)</term>, **Evil Magicians** <term>Cacomagi (from the Greek 'kakos' for bad/evil, denoting those who practice harmful sorcery)</term>, **Necromancers** <term>Necromantici (those who claim to conjure the spirits of the dead for divination)</term>, and others of this kind. Therefore, having considered these things rightly and with a good mind, we shall find that all the operations of the Devil are malignant and pernicious; they have produced no true knowledge and nothing of profit for the world, but on the contrary, a multitude of immense inconvenience, terror, constant danger, and wretched distress. The reason for this is clear, since he is the punisher <note>original: *vindex*. While often translated as 'avenger,' in this theological context, the Devil is seen as the instrument that executes punishment on those who have turned away from God.</note> of the living God and a monstrous devastator of things abandoned by God.

## C H A P T E R I.

*In which parts of the "Confession of the Fraternity of the Rosy Cross" <note>The "Confessio Fraternitatis" (1615) was the second major Rosicrucian manifesto, outlining the group's religious and philosophical beliefs.</note> the greater force and efficacy of Dr. Libavius’s <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1550–1616) was a renowned German chemist and physician who published several critiques of the Rosicrucians, viewing them as a threat to established science and religion.</note> "Analysis" or inquiry is situated. Concerning the sincere mind, intention, and goal of the author: and that it is not his intent to contradict any slanderer with contentious weapons, but rather to defend the integrity of Innocence with the shield of equity, and to wash away the stains of infamy, cast upon the Brothers of the Rosy Cross, as if with waves of truth.*

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap P with floral patterns.</image-desc>

AFTER I had traveled through Dr. Libavius’s *Analysis* <term>Analysis (an "unfolding" or detailed examination; here referring to Libavius's critical breakdown of Rosicrucian texts)</term> or inquiry into the *Confession of the Brothers of the Society of the Rosy Cross*, and had surveyed his intention with both my eyes and my mind:

<meta>catchword: -tance (from Latin "-tia")</meta>

...I perceived the greatest force and efficacy of that same analysis to be situated and centered in the examination of two of the thirty-seven arguments of the Society. For in the investigation of the first argument (which is as follows: *The Fraternity of the Rosy Cross <note>original: "Fraternitas de R. C."</note> must be approached: because the will of God, who is about to restore the world, has been declared by the signs of new stars <note>This likely refers to the "new stars" (supernovae) observed in 1572 and 1604, which many contemporaries interpreted as divine omens of a coming new age.</note> and the characters of the great book of Nature <term>Book of Nature (the idea that the physical world is a text written by God that can be "read" through science and mystical insight)</term>, etc.*), Libavius proposed two doubts to us because of the obscurity of the argument.

First: *What is this innovation and restoration of the world?*

Second: *By what proof do they justify their predictions based on new stars and characters?*

And in that place, he brought forward three assertions of the Brothers defending this same point. One was taken from page 37 of their *Confession*, which is as follows: *Secret writings and characters are useful for our predictions, but although the great book of Nature lies open to all, there are few who can read it.*

Another was also from the same page: *Our characters were inserted by God here and there in the Bible, and then also impressed quite clearly upon the heavens, the earth, and animals, etc.*

The last: *By secret characters, magical writing, and a new language, the nature of all things is declared.*

In the examination of the second argument, which seems to promise the correction and reformation of the failing arts (since almost all other matters are in some way related to this), he [Libavius] insists with marvelous ardor and vehemen- <note>The word "vehemence" is cut off by the page break.</note>

<summary>The author identifies two primary Rosicrucian arguments that Libavius critiques: the divine restoration of the world signaled by "new stars" and the existence of a mystical "Book of Nature" written in secret characters and a new language.</summary>

Therefore, the power of this argument, insofar as it extends to almost the entire confession, has sown various passions in the mind of Dr. Libavius <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1555–1616) was a prominent German physician and chemist. ->### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART I.<-

...have been, are, or shall be: let him enter our Brotherhood.
13. Whoever by the voice of his mouth, or by a musical instrument, if he desires to sing, so that he attracts not those stones of **Amphion**, but pearls and gems; not the beasts of **Orpheus**, but spirits; not **Pluto** from **Tartarus**, but the powerful princes of the world: let him enter the Brotherhood. <note>Amphion and Orpheus are figures from Greek mythology famous for their supernatural musical abilities; Amphion moved stones to build the walls of Thebes, and Orpheus charmed animals and even the King of the Underworld, Pluto, who ruled over Tartarus.</note>

Therefore, he <note>Libavius</note> denies that such secrets of Nature can be fulfilled and brought to fruition without **Diabolical** illusions and **Necromantic** deceptions. <term>Necromancy (traditionally magic involving communication with the dead, though here used more broadly for forbidden "black" magic)</term> Hence, in Section 64: *If this is how things stand, you are infamous Mages.* And Sections 65 and 66 seem to confirm this same point. Now he also mocks and derides the doctrine and wisdom of the Brothers that has been made known to the world. For his words in Section 53 are these:

*Perhaps shoemakers, tailors, and similar craftsmen will seek out your dwellings so they may learn new fashions for shoes, clothes, hats, etc., by which the noble might be distinguished from the common; in which matter, truly, if you could devise something lasting, you would be of great help. I hardly believe you can persuade barbers to cut everyone’s hair in a single style.* <note>Libavius is using biting sarcasm here, suggesting the "great wisdom" of the Rosicrucians is better suited for trivial fashion trends than for true philosophy.</note>

Frequently he strives to stain the Brothers with the mark of sedition <note>inciting rebellion against authority</note>, as in Section 28. And he seems to be disturbed by other such imaginings and the uncertain movements of an uncertain mind (having neglected the true method of **Analysis** and inquiry). Having weighed these things with care and his whole soul, [I have divided] <note>original: "dubita-" likely "dubitavi" or "dubitationibus," leading into a division of the work</note> this treatise of ours into three parts...

<summary>On this page, the author continues to document Andreas Libavius’s hostile critique of the Rosicrucian Brotherhood, specifically highlighting Libavius’s accusations of black magic and his mockery of their supposed wisdom as being fit only for common tradesmen.</summary>

...responding clearly enough to the arguments <note>The text begins mid-word with the Latin suffix "-tionibus", likely completing "argumentationibus" (arguments)</note>, objections, and suspicions of Lord <note>original: "D.", an abbreviation for *Dominus*, a title of respect for a learned man</note> Libavius, we have divided [the work], in each of which we have proposed to defend the propositions of the Brothers against the vain opinions of slanderers and their empty and frivolous reasons. In the first section of this Apology, therefore, we shall explain the various meanings of the terms **Magic** and **Cabala** <term>Cabala: a mystical system of interpretation; in this context, it refers to "Christian Cabala," which sought to find divine secrets in the letters of the Hebrew alphabet and the structure of creation</term>; then, what that great **book of Nature** is, what its writings and its characters are; and in the last place (responding to that doubt of Lord Libavius), we shall demonstrate that the predictions of the Divine Will can be declared by the signs and characters of new stars.

In the second part, we shall reveal the pollution, corruption, and defects of the arts: likewise, we will depict the necessity of reformation and the ministers to whom it has been given to correct the errors of the sciences and to restore those laboring under the rot of ignorance. And this will be the entire scope of our intention.

## C H A P T E R I I.

->*On Magic, Cabala, and Astrology.*<-

Since Lord Libavius <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1550–1616), a prominent chemist and physician who criticized the Rosicrucians for their use of "magical" language</note> in many places of his *Analysis*, seems to take the terms Magic, Cabala, and Astrology in a negative sense <note>original: "in malam partem," meaning "unfavorably" or "in a bad light"</note>, without any distinction between their various types (for in his 18th Section, *he calls Magic and Cabala detestable arts*; in Section 24, *he calls them superstitious arts along with Astrology*; in 64, *he calls the Brothers infamous Mages*, and in Section 66, *he interprets Magic absolutely as the falsest deceptions of the Devil or Necromancy*...

*understands* them <note>The text completes the sentence from the previous page: "without [Libavius] understanding [these terms] as was said above."</note> as was said above. Therefore, I have judged it useful to thoroughly examine the complete and genuine meanings and distinctions of these arts in this place, and to briefly describe their natures and properties. This serves both to express the true intention of the Brotherhood <note>The Rosicrucian Fraternity</note> and to defend its innocence against the slanders of the malevolent and the ignorant. It must be known, therefore, that if we were to utterly exclude this one species of Magic from our schools, we would be removing the most excellent portion of all natural philosophy <term>Natural philosophy: the historical study of nature and the physical universe, the precursor to modern science</term>. For this reason, it is the mark of a wise man to diligently inquire into what a thing is before he rashly condemns it. It is, therefore, the common opinion that this name "Magic" <note>original: "Magia"</note> is a Persian word, meaning the same in the Persian tongue as "wisdom" <note>original: "sapientia"</note> does among the Latins. We gather from the authority of philosophers both ancient and modern that there are various species of this, differing most clearly from one another: for one is Natural, another Mathematical, another Sorcerous <term>Venefica: traditionally "poisoning," but here referring to harmful witchcraft or the use of illicit potions</term>, another Necromantic <term>Necromantica: the practice of communicating with the spirits of the dead</term>, and another Deceptive <term>Præstigiatrix: the art of illusions, sleight of hand, or "juggling" often attributed to demonic trickery</term>. We call that most hidden and secret part of physics, by which the mystic properties of natural creatures are drawn out, "Natural [Magic]." Thus the royal wise men <note>The Three Magi or Three Kings</note> who sought the newborn Christ (with a new star from the East as their guide) were called "Magi," because they had attained the highest knowledge of natural things, both celestial <note>belonging to the heavens or stars</note> and sublunary <note>referring to the Earth and everything below the orbit of the moon</note>, they attain-

[...include]ed. <note>Completing the word "comprehensus" from the previous page.</note> Under this very category of Magi, Solomon was also included, insofar as he knew the secret powers and properties of all created things; for he is said to have understood the natures of all plants, from the cedar of Mount Lebanon even to the hyssop, etc. <note>A reference to 1 Kings 4:33, often cited in the Renaissance to portray Solomon as the ultimate natural philosopher.</note> And this species of Magic is the end toward which all natural philosophy <term>Natural philosophy: the study of nature and the physical universe before the modern era</term> is directed.

Such were the wooden dove of Archytas <note>Archytas of Tarentum (4th century BCE), a Greek mathematician credited with building the first autonomous flying machine</note> which flew, and those bronze heads of Roger Bacon <note>A 13th-century English friar and philosopher</note> and Albertus Magnus <note>A 13th-century German bishop and polymath</note>, which are said to have spoken. > *It is your purpose to know difficult things and to reveal miracles. By the ingenuity of your art, metals bellow; Diomedes sounds a trumpet more heavily in bronze; a bronze serpent hisses; simulated birds send forth their song, etc.* <note>original: "Tibi ardua cognoscere, & miracula monstrare... aves simulatæ cantilenam emittunt"</note>

Thus also I myself, by the industry of this art, have composed a wooden bull that produces moans and bellows by turns in the manner of a natural bull; a dragon moving its wings and hissing, vomiting fire and flames from its mouth onto the bull; a lyre that, by itself and without the help of a living being, plays the melody of symphonies; and many other things:

...which I confess could not have been performed by mere Mathematical art alone, without the supplement of natural Magic. <term>Veneficium</term> <note>original: "Veneficium"; literally "poisoning," but here referring to the practice of sorcery or witchcraft involving potions</note> (which is that species of Magic engaged in the use of cups, love-potions, and various medicinal sorceries) is in some way comprised under natural Magic, since the knowledge of the properties of natural things is required to carry out the effects of this species. 

Thus those Italian women who, in the manner of Circe <note>The sorceress from Homer's *Odyssey* famous for transforming men into animals</note>, are said to have turned travelers into beasts of burden by giving them a sorcerous potion in cheese, are to be held as sorceresses or Magi of that species. We also read in Philostratus <note>Flavius Philostratus (c. 170–247 AD), author of the *Life of Apollonius of Tyana*</note> that Apollonius of Tyana <note>A 1st-century Greek philosopher and reputed miracle-worker</note> used potions of this kind so that he might properly understand the voices of birds. 

<term>Necromancy</term>, which is the fourth species of Magic, they divide into <term>Goetia</term> <note>original: "Goeticam"; from the Greek word for "wailing" or "sorcery," usually implying "low" or "black" magic</note>, which is ill-omened by dealings with unclean spirits, and is fashioned from the rites of nefarious curiosity and illicit songs or incantations. Some even call upon the souls of the deceased; others are accustomed to summon evil demons adjured by the power of divine names; and this is that species of Necromancy which is called <term>Malefica</term> <note>original: "Malefica"; meaning "wicked" or "evil-doing"</note>. 

Or, it is divided into <term>Theurgy</term> <note>original: "Theurgiam"; from the Greek *theourgia*, meaning "god-working," often considered "high" or "white" magic intended to invoke divine presence</note>, which is governed—as some believe—as if by good Angels and the divine power, although it is very often performed under the names of God and Angels through the evil illusions of Demons. This part of Necromancy is perfected...

...perhaps they signified this when they spoke of artificial mirrors and the communication of one person with another across a distance of many hundreds of miles, and so forth. Therefore, it is clearer than the light of midday that the Magic of the **Society of the R.C.** <note>The Rosicrucians, a secret brotherhood of sages and alchemists whose manifestos caused a great stir in early 17th-century Europe</note> is neither superstitious, nor in any way to be detested by **Master Libavius** <note>Andreas Libavius (1555–1616), a German physician who was a vocal critic of the Rosicrucians</note> or anyone else, nor should it be considered Diabolical. Consequently, the Magi of this nature should not be hounded by hatred or by such great outcries from the malevolent; instead, they should be treated kindly, decorated with the highest honors, and duly extolled with great admiration. For into this kind of Society or cloister of Magi, **Solomon** <note>The biblical King Solomon, traditionally regarded as the ultimate master of wisdom and magic</note> himself, and those **Eastern Kings** <note>Likely referring to the Three Magi or Wise Men from the East</note>, and all those well and perfectly versed in that mystical **philosophers' stone** <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>original: "lapide illo mystico philosophorum"; the legendary substance sought by alchemists for its power to transmute metals and grant long life</note> have entered, and are numbered among the Magi of this species.

But since their **Cabala** <term>Cabala</term> <note>A system of Jewish mysticism and scriptural interpretation that explores the relationship between the eternal and the mortal</note> also suffered the same measure of infamy, and escaped the slanders of neither the envious nor the ignorant, let us examine what this Cabala actually is, and how many types of it exist. From the writings of ancient Philosophers, we gather that this Cabala is a branch of science that traces its origin back to the High Priest **Moses**. For the Jews maintain that this knowledge was handed down to Moses by divine power, and thereafter—without written records, but by a process of succession—to posterity even until the time of

...from the time of Ezra <note>original: "Esdrae"; a biblical scribe and priest credited with reviving Jewish law and tradition after the Babylonian exile</note> was continued by the living voice alone; from which it is called **Cabala** <term>Cabala</term> <note>original: "Cabala"; from the Hebrew *Qabbalah*, meaning "tradition" or "receiving"</note> by the Hebrews, as if it were the reception of something by one person from another through hearing alone. Of this, two kinds are recounted: one is called **Cosmology**, namely explaining the powers of natural created things, both earthly <note>original: "sublunarium"; literally "below the moon," referring to the changing world of the four elements</note> as well as heavenly, and setting forth the secrets of the Law and the Bible through philosophical reasoning. Likewise, he inspected the properties of beasts of burden, birds, reptiles, and fish—indeed, he thoroughly mastered the properties of all natural things through the assistance of the divine spirit. For by the virtue of this species of Cabala, he did not hesitate to bring into action those magical powers of natural things that lie hidden in the center and exist in mere potentiality.

The other distinction of Cabala is called **Mercava** <term>Merkavah</term> <note>original: "Mercava"; "Chariot" mysticism, focused on Ezekiel's vision of the divine throne-chariot and the celestial realms</note>, which concerns the more sublime contemplations of divine and angelic virtues, sacred names, and seals. And this species is divided into **Notariacon** <term>Notaricon</term> <note>original: "Notariacon"; a Kabbalistic system of shorthand where words are formed from the initial or final letters of phrases</note>, which deals universally with the virtues, names, and seals of Angels in relation to the conditions of spirits <note>original: "Daemonum"; here referring to "daemons" or intermediate spirits/intelligences rather than exclusively evil entities</note> and souls. Or it is divided into **Theomancy** <term>Theomantica</term> <note>original: "Theomanticam"; a form of magic or divination centered on the names of God</note>, which scrutinizes the mysteries of the divine Majesty and sacred names and pentacles; of which they say that he who knows it, hither...

Or why should its "breath" <note>original: "halitus"; referring to the influence or spirit of the teaching</note> be so detested by men? Or why should the **Magi** <term>Magi</term> <note>original: "Magi"; in this context, the term refers to "wise men" or "high philosophers" who study the divine secrets of nature, rather than common sorcerers</note> who embrace it be called infamous? Or if it is not so, why should its secret mysteries be held in contempt? Unless it is because those wandering in the darkness and blindness of ignorance, under the veil of that same gloom, prefer folly and worldly vanities (to speak with the mouth of Solomon) over divine wisdom, and thus desire to always remain in error. 

Should the **Brothers** <note>likely a reference to the Rosicrucian Brotherhood, often defended in such treatises</note> therefore be considered infamous and detestable Magi for the reason that, in the manner of the prophets and of **King Solomon** (whose divine and cabalistic knowledge...

...[Cabal]istic <note>original: "stica erat"; completing the word "Cabalistica" from the previous page</note> was it) who were occupied in the center and summit of the secrets and mysteries of the great book of nature? Is not the science of the true **Cabala** <term>Cabala</term> <note>original: "Cabalae"; a mystical tradition of interpreting scripture to reveal divine secrets. Renaissance thinkers like Fludd believed it was a "true science" given by God to the patriarchs.</note> divine and venerable, since it is impossible for anyone to reach its perfection without the assistance of the **Holy Spirit**, whose role it is to teach such divine mysteries and to lead men into all truth (John 14:16), provided that they faithfully and worthily invoke **God, the Best and Greatest** <note>original: "Deum optimum maximum"</note> (to whom alone it belongs to grant that Spirit to men)? (Luke 12). This was that most holy Spirit who taught **Adam**, **Enoch**, **Abraham**, **Jacob**, **Joseph**, **Moses**, **Bezalel** <note>original: "Bazaleelem"; the craftsman chosen by God in the Book of Exodus to build the Tabernacle, endowed with divine wisdom and skill</note>, **Joshua**, **David**, **Solomon**, the **Prophets**, and all the **Apostles**. Our Savior also liberally and kindly promised us that if we are to ask the Father, He will send us His good Spirit, who will teach us all things. Therefore, it is not impossible for us to arrive at the true knowledge of all things by the virtue of the divine Spirit, along with our ancestors, even though we are sinners. ...should be held <note>The text begins mid-sentence, likely continuing a thought on why certain practices are viewed with suspicion.</note>, and all the more so if it occurs through commerce with wicked **Demons**. They have noticed, of course, that false practitioners have produced a false and inconsistent science. Meanwhile, this pure and immaculate science is hidden by the ignorant under a shell of impurity; because of the world’s blindness, the divine benefits of this most noble science reveal themselves only sparingly. Therefore, the **reformation** and correction of this science must be sought above all else, since its truth has been defiled by the "leprous spots" of the unskillful and consumed by a lying decay—that is, it has been diminished and blunted, as well as polluted, by the errors of ignorance.

We conclude, therefore, from the preceding points, that insofar as the **Brothers of the Rosy Cross Society** <note>The Rosicrucians, a secret society of mystics and philosophers who claimed to possess secret wisdom.</note> are considered **Natural Mages**, **Mathematicians**, and **Kabbalists** <term>Kabbalists</term> <note>In this context, practitioners of a Christianized form of Jewish mysticism used to interpret the secrets of God and nature.</note>, they should by no right be called infamous. Rather, such a title is an injury born of the viper-like mockery of malevolent men. On the contrary, their virtues are to be admired and their **Magic** eagerly embraced, since it appears to be a true whip for ignorance, an illumination of the darkness, and [a light] for the [human] soul...

In the same way, we have distinguished their writings as being either invisible or visible. Regarding the invisible writings, which are hidden in the invisible books of God, it is not our task to debate them in this place. Whence in Genesis 1: *And he saw that the creature was good.* Indeed, this goodness—engraved by the divine characters of the Word in every creature—lay hidden in the very seed <term>spermate: the biological "seed" or germinal matter of plants, animals, or humans</term> of the creature whose generation was to be continued, even before its members were formed. My bones are not hidden from you, even though I was made in secret and formed in the depths of the earth. Your eyes saw my substance while it was still imperfect, and in your book all my members are written, which were formed little by little from day to day, when as yet they were not before.* From this, therefore, it is clear that the life-giving writing of the Word was present even in the seed of living things: because the figure, dimension, and properties of the body are weighed by God with due balances, and they receive through infallible numbers a proportion that is just and appropriate to their species. There exists (I say) this splendid writing of life even in the seminal mass of the creature, contained in the womb <term>matrice: the "matrix" or womb, where life is shaped</term> as if in the earth. For its luminous characters shine out in the darkness just as they do in the light; for in verse 11 of the aforementioned Psalm: *Darkness is not darkness to you, but the night is as clear to you as the day.* It is manifest, therefore, from these things that this writing is from the Word of the Lord, *in whom is the life of all things and without whom there was nothing.* <note>original: "in quo est vita omnium rerum & sine quo erat nihil"; an allusion to the opening of the Gospel of John</note>

For a bladder is extended and dilated by an inflated spirit or an infused substance, and through its distension, it reveals its hidden species or figure.

Indeed, they are those divine and secret seals—not of men, but offered to us by the living God and by Nature herself.

<meta>This page continues the argument from page 52 regarding the distinction between the physical, "dead" letter of written text and the "living" spiritual signs found in the natural world. It picks up the sentence fragment "higher and..." from the previous page's conclusion.</meta>

-># ON MYSTIC CHARACTERS.<-

lower- <note>completing the sentence from the previous page: "both higher and lower creatures"</note> Just as the writings of Nature are of such a kind, so too are its characters. We conclude, therefore, that characters are either killing or life-giving and spiritual; this we gather from the second letter to the Corinthians, chapter 3, verse 6: *God has made us capable ministers of the New Testament, not of the letter but of the spirit; for the letter kills, but the spirit gives life.* <note>original: "Deus nos fecit validos administratores novi Testamenti, non literâ sed spiritu; nam litera interficit, sed spiritus dat vitam."</note> From these words, it is clear that by "the letter" he means those artificial characters with which we write; but by "the spirit" he means spiritual characters, engraved supernaturally upon men and creatures at the time of creation through the Word.

For he says afterward: *You are the epistle of Christ, written not with ink, but with the spirit of the living God.* <note>original: "Estis epistola Christi scripta non cum atramento, sed cum spiritu Dei viventis."</note> By this "most holy epistle of Christ," he understands the writing of the Word, which is written not with common ink—the kind usually used to print ordinary writings—but with the spirit, as if with that most subtle and spiritual ink by which the noblest creature of all (namely, man) was sketched at the time of creation. From the life-giving power of this spirit, every creature received its virtues and essence; for "by the Word of the Lord the heavens were formed, and all their power by the Breath from His mouth," <note>A paraphrase of Psalm 33:6</note> as was said above. ...understood, they are accustomed at times to predict and foretell certain things, so that they are able to divine in a certain way regarding changes in the weather, nativities <note>Individual birth charts.</note>, theft, <term>Elections</term> <note>The astrological practice of determining the most auspicious moment to begin an undertaking.</note>, and other matters of this kind. And yet, that judgment or prognosis is quite flawed, defective, and mixed with no small amount of uncertainty and doubt, since this divination of theirs is derived from the effect alone and from experience, rather than from the origin—that is, from a sincere inspection of the characters of celestial Nature. They receive their demonstrations <term>a posteriori</term> <note>Reasoning from observed effects back to their causes.</note>, where for the most part great deception occurs, and not <term>a priori</term> <note>Reasoning from causes or fundamental principles to their inevitable effects.</note>, where all indubitable truth resides.

It is clear, therefore, that once the characters of the great **Book of Nature** are known, it will not be difficult to judge rightly and, as it were, with open eyes, concerning the effects of the stars—whether those visible since the creation or those appearing newly and recently <note>This likely refers to the "new stars" or supernovae observed in 1572 (Tycho's Star) and 1604 (Kepler's Star), which challenged the Aristotelian notion that the heavens were unchanging.</note>. Thus, ministers prepared to execute this or that matter denote the intention of the one who ordered it; so too do the characters and, as it were, the impres-

In the year of our Lord 1603, on the 20th day of the month of December, according to the new computation <note>The Gregorian calendar, which had been introduced in 1582 but was still "new" compared to the older Julian calendar.</note>, at the hour of noon, I perceived that *Saturn* was moving at 8 degrees and 39 minutes of *Sagittarius*. Similarly, I saw that *Jupiter* claimed for itself 8 degrees, 39 minutes, and 58 seconds of the same sign at almost the same instant. 

We therefore erected a celestial figure <note>A horoscope or astrological chart.</note> for that day and hour, set for a polar altitude of 38 degrees—the latitude where *Sardinia* is located. This serves as something of a midpoint for the northern latitudes known to us, counting from the Equator up to the 76th degree of elevation. ...we observed that the 27th degree of Sagittarius was placed at the cusp <note>The boundary line between two astrological houses</note>. In the 29th degree of the same sign, we found Venus. The sixteenth degree of Capricorn occupied the cusp of the eleventh house, so that the leading portion of the same sign held its natural seat. Guido Bonatus* <note>Guido Bonatti, the most celebrated astrologer of the 13th century</note> *declared through the presence of this aspect that miraculous events and wonderful things would happen in the world.* The opinions of all these authors tended toward this conclusion: namely, that the planet which is stronger and predominant in the conjunction—if it be a malefic <note>original: "infortunium"; a planet traditionally associated with hardship</note> (specifically Saturn)—would produce unhappiness and harm for the world; conversely, if it be a benefic <note>original: "fortunium"; a planet associated with good fortune</note> (namely Jupiter), it would produce happiness and growth. Therefore, having carefully pondered these things, we shall arrive at our intended purpose in this manner. In the year of our Lord 1603, on the 20th day of the month of December, according to the new reckoning <note>The Gregorian calendar, which had been introduced in 1582</note>, at the hour of noon, I perceived that Saturn was moving at 8 degrees and 39 minutes of Sagittarius. Likewise, I saw that Jupiter claimed for itself 8 degrees, 39 minutes, and 58 seconds of the same sign at almost the same instant. Therefore, we have erected a celestial figure <note>An astrological chart or "theme" calculated for a specific moment</note> for that day and hour at the polar altitude of 38 degrees (where Sardinia is situated), which represents nearly the middle of the northern latitude known to us, measuring from the Equator up to the 76th degree of elevation; for the effect of this conjunction is universal. ### APOLOGETIC TREATISE PART I. 78

[...] situated on the cusp of its own house—namely, of **Gemini** located in the **3rd house** <term>third house: the section of a horoscope traditionally governing communication, brothers, and short journeys</term>—denoting faith and the preceding religion. And I will boldly say that in many centuries we shall not find a more fitting or probable conjunction for the increase of prophecy, religion, philosophy, and wisdom than this one occurring on the year, month, and day mentioned above. Furthermore, the **new stars** <note>Referring to the Supernovae of 1572 and 1604, which the Rosicrucians interpreted as divine signs of a world-reformation</note> in those same locations of the **celestial map** <note>original: "schematis coelestis"</note> and their secret writings could undoubtedly tell us higher and more evident things, if only the true knowledge of **secret characters** <note>original: "characterum arcanorum"; the "Celestial Alphabet" where stars form letters that reveal God's will</note> were not hidden from us (as has been said). # ON MYSTIC CHARACTERS.

Furthermore, the **Brothers** know for certain that what is now hidden shall be proclaimed openly, publicly, and with a clear and eloquent voice. This will occur both through the reconciliation of the world's harmony and through the appearance of new stars in **Serpentarius** <term>Serpentarius: the constellation Ophiuchus (the Serpent-Bearer), where a famous supernova appeared in 1604</term> and **Cygnus** <term>Cygnus: the constellation of the Swan; a "new star" (P Cygni) was observed here in 1600</term>.

This is also testified by **Pisces**, the house of **Jupiter**, appearing in the ascendant <term>ascendant: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of a significant event, believed to determine the "character" of that event</term>. Since it is a fertile sign, it multiplies the things signified by its Lord <note>Jupiter, the planetary ruler of Pisces, associated here with expansion and wisdom</note>, just as was declared above. Similarly, the **Moon** moving in its own house <note>the sign of Cancer</note> and in the **fifth part of heaven** <term>fifth house: the astrological section governing children, creativity, and the "offspring" of the mind</term>—where it usually produces a most sweet effect of offspring—indicates the growth of the brothers and the increase of the "sons of wisdom" (or the effects of Jupiter). Likewise, Jupiter itself, being well-placed and fortunate, promises multiplication and accelerates the conception of the pregnant **Minerva** <note>The Roman goddess of wisdom; here used as a metaphor for the impending birth of a new age of knowledge</note>.

->ON THE IMPEDIMENTS TO THE SCIENCES.<-

...that they are born and delight in the same poison of rebellious obstinacy as those Spanish philosophers—the most foolish of all—who, immersed partly in the shadows of ignorance and partly in the fog of pride, refused the happiness of wisdom offered to them during the time of Father R. C. <note>original: "R. C." referring to Father Christian Rosenkreuz, the legendary 14th-century founder of the Rosicrucian Order.</note>; they chose instead to leave to themselves and their heirs or posterity a confused abyss of misfortune and the corruption of the world. By such contemplation, while living in this world, they might even have had familiarity with the inhabitants of heaven, loathed dark deeds, and after death passed into eternal life.

# APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.

[of the Christians] toward the unlearned, the ignorant, and the unworthy; so much so that if they have learned some secret <note>original: "arcanum"</note> pertaining to any art—whether by toilsome labor and continuous night-study from secret books, or through the documents and instructions of some faithful friend and Master—they immediately tear it away from the heart of the art now revealed. They leave it to posterity covered with only a bare skin, as it were, or a surface, while injuriously reserving its marrow <note>original: "medullam," meaning the essential core or deepest truth</note> for themselves. Or, they shroud it in such great veils, shells of words, riddles <note>original: "gryphis," referring to complex or monstrously difficult puzzles</note>, and enigmas, that among a thousand thousand men, we would scarcely find one who has ever tasted the hidden sweetness of that art.

From this it arises that the arts commonly printed and publicly produced are richer and more abundant in principles, rules, and conclusions familiar to the common people and of little consequence, than in the hidden and deeper secrets of those same arts. Hence it happens that when we intend to draw out the treasure of an art from some book attributed to this or that subject, we find nothing in it at all but trifles familiar to the common people in place of a treasure—matters far removed from the mysteries of that knowledge <note>original: "scientia"</note>. We are only able to inspect the art as far as its surface, while its most secret mysteries lie hidden and locked away toward its very center, entrusted to the custody of written or printed books, or

For among the ancients, *Socrates was unwilling for the secrets of Nature to be committed to the skins of goats and sheep <note>Socrates famously preferred oral instruction over written texts (parchment), fearing that writing would lead to a loss of true memory and allow knowledge to fall into the hands of those who could not understand it.</note> so that they might be understood by anyone*; and *Aristotle*, in his *Book of Secrets* <note>original: "libro secretorum," likely referring to the *Secretum Secretorum*, a popular medieval treatise falsely attributed to Aristotle</note> says, *that he who would communicate the secrets of art and nature would be regarded as a breaker of the celestial seal*; and the entire crowd of ancient Philosophers says, *that nothing in their philosophy has become hidden except for the secret of the art.* He says further that it is not permitted for him to reveal it to anyone, and if it were done, he would be cursed, incur the indignation of the Lord, and would die of apoplexy <note>A sudden medical catastrophe, like a stroke, interpreted here as divine punishment for revealing occult secrets.</note>. ### ON THE OBSTACLES OF THE SCIENCES.

...How might the knowledge of the properties of animals, plants, and minerals be reached? For in truth, **common Medicine**—whether it be **Galenic** <note>The traditional medical system based on the four humors (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile) as taught by the 2nd-century physician Galen.</note>, **Paracelsian** <note>The medical system of Paracelsus (1493–1541), which emphasized chemical and alchemical treatments over traditional herbal humor-based medicine.</note>, or **Astrological**—relates to the truth of science just as common **Astrology** and **Physiognomy** <note>The practice of judging a person's character or predicting their future from their outward appearance, especially the face.</note> do in the prediction of future events, in which what is done is accomplished through experience and effect. But how very often experience deceives Astrologers, deceives Physiognomists, and deceives Physicians; for the knowledge of things <term>a posteriori</term> <note>Latin: "from the latter." Reasoning that moves from observed effects back to their causes.</note> is uncertain, while it is most certain <term>a priori</term> <note>Latin: "from the former." Reasoning that moves from known causes to their inevitable effects.</note>. 

For the end or effect of a natural thing is known while the cause is ignored, since some things are more known and certain to us—namely those that are more in action and more particular—while others are known to **Nature**—namely those more universal and immaterial. Therefore, those things that are prior to Nature are confused to us, because they are more known to Nature. **Particulars very often deceive; universals never.** 

**Occult philosophy** <note>The study of the hidden (occult) forces and laws of the universe, such as magnetism, celestial influence, or the "vital spirit," which were not immediately apparent to the senses.</note> will strip nature bare, and the wisdom of universals will be contemplated by the eyes of the intellect. **Manifest [science]** is accustomed to taste from the streams of a clear fountain, yet it ignores the reason for the crystalline waters’ clarity, or their disturbance and thickness. Hence (I say) it is that sciences relying solely on experience are called uncertain and fallacious, and are to be considered spurious. From this come the errors of **Astrology**, **Physiognomy**, and **Medicine**, for...

Those more skilled in common **physiognomy** explain the lines of the face in a **Choleric** person in the same way as they do in a **Melancholic**, **Phlegmatic**, and **Sanguine** person, and thus they are most often deceived. **Astrologers** gather the positions of the visible stars at birth or during illnesses, and according to the effect of the same, they are accustomed to judging any effect of any <term>complexion</term> <note>In pre-modern medicine, "complexion" refers to the specific balance of the four humors—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—unique to an individual.</note>. Furthermore, [they tell] of a peasant digging in a field who found a golden vessel full of a noble liquor, with which he washed his face and drank from the same...

*...a small portion, and immediately he was renewed in spirit, color, and the goodness of wisdom. In medieval medicine, this referred to illnesses and symptoms that disrupted the healthy, natural state of the body.</note> occurring to the **microcosmic body** <note>The "microcosm" is a philosophical concept where the human body is viewed as a miniature version of the entire universe.</note> are corrected. This is because it has the power to restore irregular bodily humors again as if by a new effort, and to make obedient any element that is rebellious in the composition and passing beyond its limits, restoring it to its natural place. For this **celestial humor** is that bond by which the quarreling elements are united in harmonious peace within the composite body; for if this honorable medium did not exist—which passes through all elements indiscriminately and shares in each of their natures—no peace could be made between these enemies <note>The "enemies" are the conflicting qualities of the four elements (e.g., Fire is hot/dry while Water is cold/wet).</note>. Blessed, therefore, is this medicine, because it is peaceful and full of **worldly harmony**. ...to the nations. Alchemists often claimed he hid the secret of the Elixir in his writings.</note> made mention in many places (though obscurely and mystically), they left nothing at all, and understood its operations even less. A common Renaissance metaphor for exceptionally sharp or penetrating sight, referring to Lynceus, the Argonaut of myth who could see through solid objects or deep into the earth.</note>, so that they might have a correct knowledge of its composition and effect.

For **Pliny** <note>Pliny the Elder, the Roman author of the "Natural History."</note> relates that a soldier, vigorous in body and mind beyond the usual age of man, maintained his strength; when **Octavian Augustus** asked what he had done to live so long in that state, he replied enigmatically that he used **oil on the outside, and honey-wine on the inside** <note>original: "oleum posuit exteriùs, & mulsum interiùs." Mulsum was a Roman beverage made of wine and honey.</note>. Furthermore, it is told that a peasant digging in a field found a golden vessel full of a **noble liquor**, with which he washed his face and also drank...

...did they hide that secret with such cunning? Do we not require here the aid and instruction of some faithful Master, who might teach us the preparation of this medicine for the benefit of human life? For this is the one and only medicine of the entire world, by whose inspiration and property rebellious elements <note>The four elements—fire, air, water, and earth—which were believed to cause illness when they were in conflict or "rebellious" within the body.</note> are rendered obedient and tame. The Art of Alchemy or Chemistry (by whose operation that unique medicine is brought to light) is also covered by such great enigmas that we can draw nothing more from it than ignorance, or as they say, <note>original: "ignotum per ignotius"</note> explaining the unknown by means of the even more unknown.

Certainly, Master **Libavius** <note>Andreas Libavius (c. 1550–1616), a German physician and chemist. Although he was often a critic of mystical Rosicrucian claims, the author cites him here as a witness to the practical difficulties and "lies" found in chemical literature.</note> will not deny that this art requires both correction and reformation. For he himself, having spent so many years in the **Vulcanian laboratory** <term>laboratorio vulcanio</term> <note>A workshop centered around the furnace and fire, named after Vulcan, the Roman god of the forge.</note>, undoubtedly knows best through experience the difficulty of this "gilded science" and the immense and absurd lies of its writers, which he has sagaciously explored at his own expense. It is truly wonderful to notice what great invented words—so far removed from the truth of the art—the experts of this science have used, by which [they have hidden] its truth and mystery from the unworthy and the foolish (as they say)...

### ON THE IMPEDIMENTS TO THE SCIENCES. 101

...they hide <note>original: "abscondant"</note>, such as ***Sulfur*** and ***Mercury***, ***Calcination***, ***Separation***, ***Conjunction***, ***Putrefaction***, ***Congelation***, ***Fermentation***, ***Multiplication***, ***Imbibition***, ***Inceration*** <note>The process of softening a substance until it has a wax-like consistency.</note>, ***Projection***, ***Exaltation***, the ***Green Lion*** <note>A common alchemical symbol often representing raw mineral matter or corrosive acids like vitriol.</note>, ***White Smoke***, and infinite other terms of the deceptive art, by which men are deceived and persuaded to miserably squander their money. Indeed, even the more honest writers on Chemistry seem to acknowledge this in their writings: For as **Morienus** <note>Morienus Romanus, a semi-legendary 7th-century alchemist known for his dialogue with King Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> says among the philosophers: *This mastery* <term>magisterium</term> <note>The "Magistery" or Great Work; the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> *is covered and hidden, and wherever it may be, it is called by a thousand names; it is also sealed and not open except to the wise, and yet the matter is one, and the way of operation is unique and straightforward.*

And (as it seems to us), neither the common fire of the public but that of Nature, nor artificial furnaces but natural vessels and matrices <note>Natural "wombs" or environments where growth occurs.</note> are required in this work; since it is the work of Nature alone, in which nothing is required except the small aid of a servant, by which natural things may be rightly administered to natural things of a matching species. 

We conclude, therefore, that in the sciences of **Natural Philosophy**, **Medicine**, and **Alchemy** as they are commonly accepted and made known to men, a reformation and correction (if it can be done in any way) must be sought and expected; therefore it would be absurd, and deserving of punishment by the lash of ignorance, to rashly or negligently reject masters who propose a method of correction to us; For it will not be of a more advanced...
