APOLOGETIC TREATISE PART II. 102

...even for men of a more advanced age <note>original: "ætatis hominibus"</note>, it is no disgrace in these times—in which ignorance holds its dominion—to rectify their minds and to refresh and restore them with the balsam of wisdom. Let us not (I say) be reluctant or weary because we have begun poorly, but let us instead start over with a more certain and better order, since the path to good morals and true wisdom is never too late. Let us eagerly see (I say), whether *the Philosophy of the Fraternity is the head, sum, foundation, and completion of all faculties, sciences, and arts*, as they themselves assert on page 58 of the *Confession* <note>The *Confessio Fraternitatis* (1615) was the second anonymous manifesto of the Rosicrucian Order, calling for a global transformation of science and religion.</note>. If this is so: without a doubt, the defects of the old philosophy can be corrected and reformed by this same new philosophy, and this Society is to be rewarded with the highest honors; their Fame should be eternally preserved and committed to the most fortunate books.

...they found by observation, they **added and joined to Mathematics, Physics, and Magic.** <note>original: "observatione invenerunt... addiderunt atque annexuerunt." The author is quoting the Fama Fraternitatis, which claims that secret wisdom was shared and refined across borders.</note>

Almost the same plague and contagion <note>The author uses medical metaphors like "plague" (lue) and "contagion" to describe the stagnation and corruption of traditional academic learning.</note> weakens that branch of the sciences which is called Mathematical. For in every <term>Mathematical</term> art there are the greatest defects and huge obstacles. For as in the first place, proceeding methodically, let us examine <term>Arithmetic</term> <note>From the Greek *arithmos* (number); the study of numbers and their properties.</note>, and look back at the writings of mathematicians for its effectiveness, we will find in them many technical terms, almost innumerable principles, many theoretical operations, and almost infinite conclusions or practices quite familiar to the common people.

In the first place, therefore, we find the *method of Adding, Subtracting, Multiplying, Dividing, and Progressing described clearly enough for us.* Similarly, the *Golden Number of Integers* <note>original: "Numerum aureum Integrorum." While the "Golden Number" usually refers to the 19-year lunar cycle used to calculate Easter, here it likely refers to fundamental "golden" rules or ratios used in whole-number calculations.</note>, *fractions, the extraction of the Square and Cube Roots,* and other such common rudiments of <term>Arithmetic</term> useful to Merchants, Treasurers, and <term>Geometers</term> <note>Those who study the measurement of shapes and spaces.</note> engaged in <term>Altimetry</term> <note>The science of measuring heights.</note>, <term>Planimetry</term> <note>The science of measuring plane surfaces (areas).</note>, the triple dimension of bodies, and other common operations of <term>Geometry</term>. Similarly, they are helpful to those more skilled in military affairs <note>original: "re militari." Mathematics was essential for 17th-century warfare, particularly for calculating the trajectories of artillery and the layout of fortifications.</note>, by which they may organize their cohorts by natural progression into a Triangular or Rhomboidal figure, or [extract?] the root...

### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.
<meta>catchword: Cossic</meta>

...by root extraction, they reduce them into the form of a square battle-array. <note>In the early modern period, "extracting the square root" was a practical skill used by military officers to arrange a specific number of soldiers into a perfect square formation for battle.</note> Similarly, there are things conducive to Musicians, by which they diligently investigate the proportions of the musical Systems and Times, so that they may compose symphonic music <note>original: "cantum symphoniacum." This refers to polyphony—music with multiple harmonizing parts—rather than a modern orchestral symphony.</note> more readily. Hence, they are also superficially and moderately proficient in common proportions, from which it follows that they know how to distinguish proportions of equality from those of *inequality; and among inequalities, the major from the minor; and among the Major, the simple from the composite; and among the simple, the Multiples from the superparticulars and superpartients; and among the Composites, the multiple-particular from the multiple-superpartient.* <note>The author is using the complex classification system of ratios inherited from the Roman philosopher Boethius. These ratios defined both mathematical relationships and musical intervals.</note>

*They know, therefore, what is a double proportion (2:1), triple (3:1), quadruple (4:1), and so on. What is a sesquialtera (3:2), sesquitertia (4:3), sesquiquarta (5:4), etc.* <note>These are "superparticular" ratios where the larger number contains the smaller plus one part of it (e.g., 3 contains 2 plus half of 2). In music, 3:2 is a perfect fifth.</note> *What is a superbipartient (5:3), supertripartient (7:4), and others of this kind. What is a double sesquialtera (5:2), and sesquiquarta (9:4), a triple sesquitertia (10:3), and sesquiquinta (16:5), a quadruple sesquialtera (9:2) and sesquisexta (25:6). What is a double superbipartient (8:3) and supertripartient (11:4), a triple superbipartient (11:3) and supertripartient (15:4), etc. Similarly, regarding minor inequality, which is simple and which is composite. And so on for the rest.*

And in order to make this art appear more wonderful, and to surpass the ancients in their inventions, some of the <term>neoterics</term> <note>From the Greek *neoteros* (newer); a term used for modern "innovators," often with a hint of skepticism by traditionalists.</note> have devised a new formula of Arithmetic, and an unheard-of method of operating the same, distinct from the old one in its numbers and characters, which [they call] **Cossic** <note>original: "Arithmeticam Cofficam." From the Italian "cosa" (thing), this was the early name for Algebra, where the unknown variable was called "the thing."</note>

# ON THE OBSTRUCTIONS OF THE SCIENCES.

...they have named the <term>Cossic Art</term> <note>original: "Cofficam" (Cossic). From the Italian 'cosa' (the thing), this was the early modern term for Algebra, where the unknown variable was called 'the thing.'</note>. And although it is composed of characters and signs representing Geometric figures—namely the *Root*, the *Square*, the *Cube*, the *Biquadrate* <note>original: "Quadratum de Quadratis," or the square of squares (x⁴).</note>, and others of this kind—as well as the differences of *Plus* and *Minus*, they have nevertheless never been able to reach the wisdom and doctrine of the ancients by this invention of theirs. That ancient wisdom was bounded by the true art of Arithmetic and hidden, as it were, by proportional clouds; for it seems that the secrets of the ancients in *Arithmetic* have been devoured by the teeth of oblivion, or rather of envy, or at least are revealed and uncovered to only a very few. 

Whether this occurred because of the <term>Pythagorean silence</term> <note>The followers of Pythagoras were famously sworn to secrecy regarding their mathematical and philosophical discoveries, punishing those who revealed secrets like the existence of irrational numbers.</note>, or on account of the theft committed by ancient Philosophers who snatched away the very marrow of this science and hid its profound mysteries from common eyes. Some elements of Arithmetical proportions have indeed been revealed to men (as has been said), but where shall we find their true uses and effects? For it is in the center of those effects that the most hidden and divine secrets of all *Arithmetic* lie concealed. 

We use these proportions superficially in *Geometric* and *musical* exercises, but it is not the custom of our age to inquire more deeply into their essence. On the contrary, those who appear to be more deeply engaged in this art are held to be impious and wicked <term>Mages</term>, especially if they should publicly produce the wonderful effects of this science; and in this way...

->### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.<-

...by extraction, they reduce them into a form or a square battle formation <note>Early modern military theorists used arithmetic to calculate "tactical squares," determining how many soldiers were needed to form a square block for defense or maneuvers.</note>. Similarly, these numbers are useful to Musicians, by which they diligently investigate the proportions of the Musical System and of Time, so that they may compose symphonic song more promptly. From this, they are proficient—at least on the surface and to a moderate degree—in common proportions, from which it happens that they know how to distinguish *proportions of equality from those of inequality, and the greater from the lesser inequalities, and the simple major proportions from the compound, and the simple Multiples from the Superparticulars and Superpartients; and the Compound Multiple-Particular from the Multiple-Superpartient.* <note>These terms follow the Boethian system of ratios. A "Multiple" is a whole number ratio (e.g., 2:1); a "Superparticular" is a ratio where the larger number is the smaller plus one (e.g., 3:2); and a "Superpartient" is a ratio where the larger number is the smaller plus more than one (e.g., 5:3).</note> 

They know, therefore, what a *double, triple, and quadruple proportion, etc.,* is. What is a *sesquialtera, sesquitertia, sesquiquarta, etc.* <note>original: "sesquialtera" (3:2), "sesquitertia" (4:3), "sesquiquarta" (5:4). These ratios define musical intervals like the perfect fifth and fourth.</note> What is a *superbipartiens, supertripartiens, and others of this kind.* <note>original: "superbipartiens" (5:3), "supertripartiens" (7:4).</note> What is a *double sesquialtera and sesquiquarta, triple sesquitertia and sesquiquinta, quadruple sesquialtera and sesquisexta.* What is a *double superbipartiens and supertripartiens, triple superbipartiens and supertripartiens, etc.* Similarly, *which minor inequality is simple, and which is compound. And so on for the rest.* 

And so that they might make this art more miraculous and excel the ancients in their own inventions, some of the moderns have fashioned a new formula of Arithmetic and an unheard-of method of operating the same, distinct from the ancient numbers and characters, which [they call] <term>Cossic</term> Arithmetic <note>original: "Arithmeticam Cofficam." This refers to early algebra. The term "Cossic" comes from the Italian "cosa" (the thing), used to represent the unknown variable 'x'.</note>...

# ON THE HINDRANCES TO THE SCIENCES.

G 5

...they have called it the **Cossic** art <note>The "Cossic" art (from the Italian *cosa*, meaning "thing") was the early Renaissance term for algebra, where the unknown variable was called the "thing."</note>. And although it is composed of characters and signs representing **Geometric figures**—namely the **Root**, the **Square**, the **Cube**, the **Biquadrate** <note>original: "Quadratum de Quadratis," literally the square of squares, or the fourth power ($x^4$).</note>, and other such things, as well as the differences of **Plus** or **Minus**—yet they were never able to reach the wisdom and teaching of the ancients through this invention of theirs. That ancient knowledge was bounded by the true art of **Arithmetic** and hidden, as if by proportional clouds. For it seems that the secrets of the ancients in **Arithmetic** have been devoured by the teeth of oblivion, or rather of envy, or at least are revealed and uncovered only to a few. This may have been because of the **Pythagorean silence** <note>The followers of Pythagoras were famously sworn to secrecy, under penalty of death, not to reveal mathematical discoveries (like irrational numbers) to the uninitiated.</note>, or because of the theft by ancient philosophers who snatched away the marrow of this science and hid its profound mysteries from common eyes.

Some elements of **Arithmetic Proportions** have been revealed to men (as has been said), but where shall we find their true uses and effects? In their very center, the most hidden and divine secrets of all Arithmetic lie concealed. We use these proportions only superficially in **Geometric** and **Musical** exercises; indeed, it is not the custom of our age to inquire more deeply into their essence. On the contrary, those who seem to occupy themselves more profoundly in this art are held to be impious and wicked **Mages** <note>original: "Magis," the plural of "Magus." The author is lamenting that advanced mathematics was often mistaken for sorcery or "black" magic in the eyes of the public.</note>, and all the more so if they publicly produce the admirable effects of that same art; and in this way...

Who among us today can discover those **living numbers** by which the **elements** are linked and bound to one another? In this way, that mystical numerical science called "Pythagorean" is being uprooted and extinguished. It would therefore be a benefit of the highest order to the world and the arts to restore the secrets of this most noble science—now almost extinct and extirpated—and to revive and repair this wasting art.

**Music** follows **Arithmetic** in the same way that **Medicine** follows **Natural Philosophy** <term>Natural Philosophy: The study of nature and the physical universe, the precursor to modern science.</term>; in which science it is a familiar and common practice of the art *to diligently examine the harmonic systems invented by human art, as well as the keys—both the principal ones, namely the round and the square <note>original: "rotundam nempe & quadratam." ...to find [the sounds] marked and understood within them: and to demonstrate the proper places of the voices, notes, or syllables—*Do, Re, Mi, Fa, Sol, La* <note>original: "Vt. Re. Mi. Fa. Sol. La." These are the Guidonian syllables used in solmization; "Ut" was the original name for "Do."</note>—so that one might properly produce the sounds from which melody is made. Similarly, to measure with compasses the properties of the <term>monochord</term> <note>A scientific and musical instrument with a single string and a movable bridge, used to demonstrate the mathematical ratios of musical intervals.</note>, distinguished by intervals and the consonances derived from them, so that concordant sounds in symphonic harmony may be distinguished from discordant ones. Also, to express the proportions and values of musical time with characters or notes: the *Maximal*, *Long*, *Breve*, *Semibreve*, *Minim*, *Semiminim*, *Fusa* <note>These terms refer to the "mensural notation" used before the modern system. Finally, [the goal is] to understand the differences of consonances and their linking together: both the perfect ones, such as the *unison*, the *octave* <note>original: "Diapason"</note>, and the *fifth* <note>original: "Diapente"</note> (as well as those arising from them), and the imperfect ones, such as the *fourth* <note>original: "Diatessaron"</note>, and that "sixth chord," and others derived from them. One must understand their use and nature in musical practice, by which they might modulate a "Symphony"—that is, a concordant harmony of distinct sounds and melodies, and a fitting agreement.

But, good God, what is all this compared to the true and profound music of the wise man? By that music, the proportions of natural things are investigated, the harmonic consensus and properties of the whole world are revealed, and even the joints [of the universe] are bound together...

### PART II OF THE APOLOGETIC TREATISE ###

...the peace of the struggling elements is sealed; each star, by its own weight, virtue, and the proportion of its luminous soul, is perpetually suspended in its own proper place, according to that saying of *Mercurius Trismegistus*: <note>original: "Mercurij Trismegisti" — Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the *Hermetica* (ancient texts of Egyptian-Greek wisdom) are attributed.</note> *Distinct things are balanced by the fiery spirit that carries them.* By what proportion of common music is the spiritual matter of the solar body found relative to its luminous form, so that it might—through the most perfect material consonance of the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The Greek term for an octave interval. In this context, it represents a perfect mathematical ratio that bridges different realms.</note> reaching downward—benevolently strike the earth? Or why does the star of Saturn, extending itself downward toward the element of fire by the consonance of the Diapason, sow such great disturbances, alterations, and deadly seeds throughout the entire elemental region? For this is why, although this planet is good in itself, it is nonetheless deadly to living things by reason of its discordant harmony. Similarly, why is Mars also considered a misfortune, if not because of the extension of its consonance of the Diapason toward an element contrary to its nature—namely, Water? Meanwhile, Jupiter is considered a fortunate star for the race of living beings bec[ause...]

# III

## ON THE OBSTACLES TO THE SCIENCES.

...due to the relation of that <term>Diapason</term> <note>original: "Diapason" — the Greek term for an octave interval, representing a perfect mathematical harmony.</note> to the life-giving element suited to its nature—namely, to Air, which relates to fire and water just as Jupiter relates to Mars and Saturn. An exact knowledge of these things (I say) cannot be obtained without the contemplation and revelation of the hidden science of Music, which the unfairness of the times or the abyss of envy have nearly swallowed up.

How, then, shall we finally arrive at the most beautiful structure of the <term>microcosmic Palace</term> <note>original: "Palatij microcosmici" — A philosophical concept viewing the human being as a "little world" (microcosm) that mirrors the structure of the entire universe (macrocosm).</note>? By what bonds of hidden Music is that excellent essence of man bound within his body? Or how is it possible for that essence—by means of the <term>ethereal spirit</term> <note>original: "spiritu æthereo" — In ancient physiology, a subtle, light-filled substance thought to act as the intermediary between the immaterial soul and the heavy, physical body.</note>—to descend into a subject and dwelling so opposite to it (as light is to darkness) and so remote from its natural home, namely into an opaque body, and within it produce a music delightful enough for the world? 

Is it even possible for anyone to respond directly to this question without the proper contemplation of this sacred and divine Music? Happy, therefore, will be the one who is well-versed in such mysteries of hidden Music, since without the knowledge of these things it is impossible for one to know oneself. Unless this knowledge of self occurs, one cannot reach the perfect knowledge of God; for he who truly and inwardly understands himself will perceive within himself the idea of the divine <term>Trinity</term> and the true characters of the Word from which he was

Indeed, the music commonly known and used among us relates to that deep and secret music of Nature no differently than whiteness relates to a wall, or a surface to a solid body; for instrumental or vocal music is used by men only because a shadow of pleasure seems to soothe their ears; but <term>mundane music</term> <note>original: "mundana" — The "music of the spheres" or the mathematical harmony of the cosmos.</note> and <term>human music</term> <note>original: "humana" — The internal harmony between the human soul and body.</note> are utterly neglected and ignored, by which the human soul can be sublimated and exalted to the seat of its creator.

The author argues we focus only on the lowest, audible form.</note> are utterly neglected and unknown—yet it is by these that the human soul can be sublimated and exalted to the seat of its creator.

If we examine Geometry, as it is currently used among us, with the necessary inquiry, we will find the writings of Euclid <note>Euclid of Alexandria (c. 300 BC), the Greek mathematician often called the "founder of geometry."</note> filled with many principles, maxims, propositions, and theorems. Therefore, through this commonly used art, we are taught to describe regular figures—such as *the three types of triangles, triple squares, and the circle—as well as irregular ones of many species, such as trapezoidal figures, ovals, and others of this kind; and to delineate them both on surfaces and also within bodies enclosed by three dimensions:* Next, we consider certain propositions or theorems serving at times architecture, some the motion of machines, and others <term>Altimetry: the science of measuring heights.</term> altimetry, <term>Planimetry: the measurement of plane surfaces.</term> planimetry, and similar pursuits. Indeed, we find certain measuring instruments that measure the distance from a starting point to a destination using a single or multiple station <note>This refers to the practice of surveying from fixed points of observation.</note>.

And all these things are known and observed by almost anyone skilled in this art. But how far all these things are from the true perfection of Geometry and the knowledge of its wondrous secrets, I leave to the judgment of all of you who are curious in this science, by whom the machines of Archimedes <note>Archimedes of Syracuse (c. 287–212 BC), a Greek polymath whose legendary inventions included the "claw" and heat rays used to defend Syracuse from Roman ships.</note> and their stupendous effects are known. For by their power and efficacy, he used to lift the huge ships of the enemies upward with incredible violence, and he performed many other wonders through this art that are now unknown to us. Where—

If we examine the Geometry currently in use among us with the proper investigation, we will find the writings of *Euclid* <note>Euclid of Alexandria (c. 300 BC) was a Greek mathematician often referred to as the "founder of geometry" for his work *Elements*.</note> filled with many principles, maxims, propositions, and theorems. We are taught to delineate them both on surfaces and in bodies enclosed by three-fold dimensions <note>Length, width, and depth.</note>. Next, we consider certain propositions or *Theorems*, some serving *Architecture*, some the movement of machines, and some serving *Altimetry* <note>The measurement of heights.</note>, *Planimetry* <note>The measurement of plane surfaces.</note> and similar applications. Indeed, will we find any man so skilled in Architecture in this century of ours who knows how to build a **Colossus** like that of Rhodes, whose height was 70 cubits? <note>The Colossus of Rhodes was a statue of the Greek sun-god Helios, one of the Seven Wonders of the Ancient World.</note> Where now shall we find that craftsmanship of **Archytas**, by which a dove made of wood is said to have flown in the air? <note>Archytas of Tarentum (c. 400 BC) was a mathematician and statesman reputed to have built the first artificial, self-propelled flying machine.</note> Or by what means were those **bronze heads** of **Roger Bacon** and **Albertus Magnus** manufactured, both of which they claim spoke? <note>Roger Bacon (c. 1214–1292) and Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280) were medieval scholars associated with legends of "talking heads"—mechanical or magical automata that could answer questions.</note> And in what forgotten fissures is that **Mathematical Magic of Boethius** <note>Anicius Manlius Severinus Boethius (c. 477–524 AD), a philosopher whose works on the quadrivium (arithmetic, music, geometry, and astronomy) were foundational to medieval education.</note> hidden? By his genius, metals would low <note>original: "mugiebant" — to bellow like cattle.</note>, **Diomedes** would blow a trumpet more gravely in bronze, a bronze snake would hiss, and he used to perform other things of this kind, to the admiration of all who were present. By what way, finally, did the **Egyptian priests** perform things so astounding and wonderful to the common observer? Concerning this art, therefore, **Plato** seems to understand when he speaks thus in the eleventh [book] of *The Laws*:

> *Art was given to mortals so that they might generate certain secondary things, not indeed partaking of truth and divinity, but so they might derive certain likenesses known to themselves;*

and hence it was, in truth, that the Philosophers called **art the ape of Nature.** <note>The phrase "Ars simia Naturae" refers to the idea that human art and technology attempt to imitate the creative power and mechanisms of the natural world.</note> In the Geometric art, therefore, there is not only a need for correction in some things, but also a restoration of its lost treasures.

...the two **Colures** <note>original: "Coluros"; these are two principal great circles of the celestial sphere that intersect at the poles and pass through the equinoxes or solstices</note>. They denote that all of these are parallel to each other and to the **Equator**, and are consequently "direct" <note>meaning they run parallel to the primary daily motion of the heavens rather than at an oblique angle</note>. They are also accustomed to investigate the sites or positions of the **fixed stars**, their longitudes and latitudes both within the **Zodiac** and outside it, as well as their commonly known natures.

In the same way, they know the positions of the planets, including which circles—both **eccentric** <note>circles whose center is offset from the Earth</note> and **concentric**—they belong to. They attribute **Epicycles** <note>small circles whose centers move around the circumference of larger circles, used in the Ptolemaic system to explain planetary orbits</note> to them, along with their triple motion: namely, **direct**, **double stationary**, and **retrograde** <note>the apparent backward motion of a planet as seen from Earth</note>; these are attributed to all the planets except the Sun. They recognize both their "violent" and "snatched" motion <note>original: "motum violentum, & raptum"; the rapid daily rotation of the heavens from east to west</note>, namely from east to west, and their "natural" motion, by which they move from west to east. Similarly, they acknowledge their vibration and **trepidation** <note>a historical astronomical theory suggesting a slow oscillation in the position of the equinoxes</note>, specifically across the latitude of the world from the **ecliptic** <note>the path of the Sun</note> toward the poles; they say that the planets originally received this property, along with the fixed stars, from the arrangement and nature of the **eighth heaven** <note>in the medieval geocentric model, the sphere containing the fixed stars</note>.

Those skilled in **spurious Astrology** <note>the author refers to common or superstitious astrology as "spurious" to distinguish it from a more rigorous "true" science</note> also know why this planet, or that sign, is called **northern**, **southern**, **western**, or **oriental** because of its position. Thus also, they know that any planet and any sign, in respect to their **complexions** <term>complexions: the specific mixture of the four primary qualities (hot, cold, wet, dry) that defines a celestial body's physical and astrological nature</term>, may be called hot, cold, moist, or dry; fiery, airy, watery, or earthy; masculine, feminine, diurnal, nocturnal, mobile, fixed, or common...

### TREATISE ON THE APOLOGY, PART II.

...I have perceived this as if with illuminated eyes; namely, that those who understand almost nothing or very little, nevertheless boast that they know everything. On the contrary, we have seen very few, or plainly none in this our age, who are truly experienced in this science, while meanwhile a quite large number of **false professors** and those utterly ignorant in this art are found everywhere, who promise much but deliver little or nothing. The reason for this is that this is the <term>Cabalistic art<gloss>referring here to a tradition of secret or mystical knowledge, often associated with Jewish mysticism but here applied to the hidden mechanics of the universe</gloss></term>, first explained to the elders and ancestors of the **Jews**, by whose power they produced wonders in the divination of the future and the inspection of the past, as is more broadly declared by **Josephus** in his history of the Jews. <note>Flavius Josephus, a 1st-century Romano-Jewish historian, claimed in his *Antiquities of the Jews* that the descendants of Seth and Abraham were the original masters of astronomy and astrology.</note> 

We are also taught that the essence <note>original: "medullam," literally the marrow</note> of this science was first handed down to **Adam** by divine will, and successively flowed down to others. If this science is known correctly and perfectly, it is without a doubt as certain in its operations as the motion of the heavens and the stars themselves as they **aspect** <note>original: "invicem aspicientium," referring to the geometric angles or "aspects" planets make to one another, which determine their influence</note> one another is certain. Indeed, the effects of the stars upon these lower things are as certain as it is certain that God has ordered all things which are in the world. 

Nor is it to be believed that the **Devil** himself knew future things by any other knowledge (since, as **Holy Scripture** testifies, he shares nothing of the secrets of the mind) <note>A common theological argument that only God can know a person's private thoughts; the Devil must rely on external signs and physical laws.</note> except insofar as he learned all events from the movements of the stars; as if he saw them described in golden letters in the ethereal fields—

For to them, the measure of the heavens and their height, together with the number of the Stars, was well known, inasmuch as they first obtained the science of these things from God. They afterwards made this science <term>Cabalistic<gloss>from the Hebrew "Qabbalah," meaning "received tradition"; here referring to an oral, esoteric transmission of divine secrets rather than the later Jewish mystical system</gloss></term>, always handing it down successively to posterity, as fathers do to their sons. 

There are, however, some who have wished the secret of this science to lie hidden in the true nature and motion of the signs of a certain **invisible Zodiac** directly towering above the **visible Zodiac**. They say that the invisible Zodiac is in the ninth heaven, just as the visible one is in the eighth; from this, they conclude that various effects are produced in the world because of the diverse operations of the one invisible Zodiac—namely, its influence upon the visible one.

# APOLOGETIC TREATISE: PART II.

## H

...which could not have happened unless the motions of the higher and lower <note>In this context, the "higher" refers to the archetypal or invisible Zodiac, and the "lower" refers to the visible, physical stars we see in the sky.</note> differed from each other. For in the beginning of the world, the first degree of **Aries** <term>Aries<gloss>The first sign of the Zodiac, traditionally representing the start of Spring and the moment of the world's creation.</gloss></term> in the invisible Zodiac stood directly above the first degree of that visible **Aries**. From this it happened that that age was "golden" and free from strife, because the same element did not attack its own likeness through contrary actions. It was for this reason that the world had its origin alongside **Paradise**, etc.

But because the movements of both Zodiacs were different, a variety of conditions was imposed upon worldly things. For the wise have observed that the invisible Zodiac moved by a single degree and 28 minutes in every 100 years, and consequently completed its revolution in 49,000 years—a span of time they called a **Platonic Year** <note>original: "annum Platonicum." While modern astronomy calculates the cycle of precession at about 26,000 years, various esoteric traditions assigned different lengths to this "Great Year," after which the stars return to their original positions.</note>. The **Platonists** write that once this full revolution is completed, all things exist in the same state and disposition as they were at the start of the revolution.

However, the period of the world (as it seems to me, if there is any probability in this opinion) should rather be assigned to the place opposite the invisible **Aries**, since in that place is located the Sun’s <term>depression, detriment, and fall<gloss>Astrological terms for signs where a planet's power is most weakened; for the Sun, this is Libra, the sign opposite Aries.</gloss></term>, upon whose power all creatures depend. Therefore, with the Sun languishing and weakened, it would be necessary for the lives of things depending on it—and even the life of the entire world—to languish, be deprived, and perish; if nature’s...

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the shifting relationship between the two Zodiacs. ON THE HINDRANCES OF THE SCIENCES. 129

...let us look at the order only. Similarly, *Timocharis* <note>Timocharis of Alexandria (c. 320–260 BCE) was a Greek astronomer whose observations of the stars were later used by Hipparchus to discover the precession of the equinoxes.</note> (who lived in the time of *Alexander* [the Great]) noted from an observation of the *Pleiades* <note>The Pleiades are a prominent star cluster in the constellation Taurus, often used by ancient navigators and astronomers for timekeeping.</note> that the eighth heaven <note>In the ancient geocentric model, the "eighth heaven" or eighth sphere was the realm of the "fixed stars," situated beyond the spheres of the seven known planets.</note> moves nearly one degree every hundred years. From this, it is clear that the motion of the lower heaven is faster than that of the higher. 

This is the reason why the infinite degrees of distance between the "head" <note>The "head" refers to the starting point or 0 degrees of a zodiac sign.</note> of the invisible **Aries** and that of the visible one produce effects for infinite worldly occurrences, according to the various natures of the degrees or parts of the higher and lower signs. Thus, **Aries** of the lower [Zodiac], insofar as it is a fiery sign, being positioned under a sign contrary to its nature—for instance, under **Cancer**, which is watery—envelops the lower world and produces both watery and fiery disturbances simultaneously. 

When positioned under **Taurus**, it procreated earthy effects; under **Gemini**, airy effects; under **Leo**, fiery and intensely hot effects, and so on. In this way, as the <term>triplicity<gloss>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element: Fire, Earth, Air, or Water.</gloss></term> of the lower heaven moves under a triplicity of the higher heaven that is contrary to it, it has scattered disturbances of opposing natures: such as fire under water, or water under fire, or earth under air, etc. 

Furthermore, masculine degrees placed under feminine ones, bright degrees under dark ones, and other such combinations will contribute much to these effects. From this, it is evident that a wise person, through these kinds of observations, can judge the disposition and nature of any age and perform wonders—provided that the certain and precise motion of these **Zodiacs** is found by a suitable calculation...

**Saturn**, by its dominance in our hemisphere, makes the world more sorrowful and more prone to <term>melancholy</term> <note>Melancholy was considered a physical "humor" or fluid (black bile) that caused sadness, but also deep intellectual focus.</note>. **Mars** makes men litigious <note>Prone to arguing or legal disputes.</note>, just as **Venus** makes them prone to <term>luxury</term> <note>In this context, "luxury" refers specifically to physical pleasure and lust.</note> and the allurements of the flesh; and so it is with the others.

### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.

...exchange was devised by men, as was the first bold attempt at sailing, and many other things of that kind. After the dominion of Mercury, the **spirit of Mars** <note>In this chronological system, the spirit of Mars is Samael. He is associated with conflict, drastic change, and the movement of nations.</note> began to reign, from the year 1417 until the year 1771 and the 8th month. Under his dominion, according to the **Hebrews**, the universal flood occurred in the year 1656. Philosophers have observed that as often as the spirit of Mars has held dominion in the world in its series and succession, so often has a significant and notable alteration of **Monarchies** arisen. Religions and sects are also varied under him, laws are changed, and princedoms and kingdoms are transferred to foreigners—which we can easily find through the historical records in their proper order.

That **Angel of the Moon** <note>Known as Gabriel. In astrology and medieval science, the Moon governs growth, moisture, and the foundational elements of society.</note> succeeded the Martial spirit in the worldly kingdom, beginning his reign from the 8th month of the year 1771 and lasting in his vigor until the year 2126. Under him, men were multiplied and they established various cities. Under the dominion of this spirit—namely in the year 2242—**Isidore** <note>Isidore of Seville (c. 560–636 AD), a scholar whose encyclopedic works were standard references in the Middle Ages.</note> and **Bede** <note>The Venerable Bede (c. 672–735 AD), an English monk and historian famous for his works on chronology and time-reckoning.</note> maintain that the flood took place. This is in opposition to the Hebrews, who maintain it happened in the year 2656 <note>original: "Anno 2656." This is a discrepancy in the source text, which earlier cited the Hebrew date for the flood as 1656.</note>. ### ON THE IMPEDIMENTS TO KNOWLEDGE.

### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.

...he must produce fruits worthy of repentance, by praying, fasting, and giving alms; for unless he does these things, the Holy Spirit will not come. After repentance, he must not fall back into sins, but must instead abstain from all luxury, gluttony, drunkenness, and shamelessness, and from all levity and impurity. He must invoke God without ceasing and pray with a firm and undoubting faith for the granting of the Holy Spirit. 

This is how our ancestors acted: they observed fasts, drank no wine, and ate no meat. Thus did **Ezra** and **Daniel**; indeed, even **Moses** himself, before he approached God, stayed away from his wife for three days. They washed their garments and performed every act according to the divine command, as we read in **Exodus 19** <note>original: "2. Moyſes 19". <header>APOLOGETIC TREATISE PART III.</header>

...we will pass lightly and superficially <note>The author suggests he is skipping over complex metaphysical details to get to the practical "effects" of nature.</note> so that we might express the more wonderful effects of Light and of spirit. It must therefore be known that God, the **Being of Beings**, the eternal form, most holy, purely fiery and loving, without any mixture of matter, did not manifest Himself before the creation of the world, according to that saying of **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure believed to be the author of the Hermetic Corpus, often cited in Renaissance philosophy as a source of ancient wisdom.</note>: *The Monad generates a monad, and reflects its own ardor back into itself* <note>original: "Monas generat monadem, & in se-ipsum reflectit ardorem suum"</note>, and Psalm 18, verse 12: *And he made darkness his hiding place round about him, his pavilion dark waters in thick clouds, etc.*

But indeed, when it was His will to found the **macrocosm** <term>macrocosm: The "great world" or the entire universe, viewed as a structured whole.</term> in the lap or womb of *hyle* <term>hyle: A Greek word for "matter." In this context, it refers to the "prime matter"—the raw, chaotic substance of the universe before God gave it form.</term> which was still formless (for darkness was upon the face of the infinite Abyss), to speak with Trismegistus, *He changed His form and suddenly revealed all things, and all things were turned into light, most sweet.* And a little later, *A certain horrific shadow slipped beneath in an oblique revolution* or as in Psalm 18:12: *at the brightness that was before him, dark and thick clouds passed.*

For God produced the word (**FIAT** <note>Latin for "Let there be" or "Let it be done," referring to the divine command in Genesis.</note>) and the Spirit, proceeding from both, was carried in a motion that traced a circle—which is the most capacious figure in Geometry—within the lap of the infinite prime matter. By this motion, the containment and circumference of the finite macrocosm were described; and thus the Spirit, which was previously unknown and (through the privative power of darkness) devoid of all form, now [takes on] the form of waters by the power of the brilliance of that divine Spirit...

# APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.

<header>APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART II.</header>
<meta>catchword: whence</meta>

[This higher region] consists of three parts of radiant form and only one part of water, which is the purest spirit of all created things. For this reason, it is superexcellent and almost entirely "formal" <note>original: "formalis"; in this philosophical context, it refers to the presence of "Form," the active, spiritual principle that gives shape and life to passive matter.</note>. Its appetite is abundantly satisfied, and it is intoxicated, as it were, by a fiery and divine nectar. In the same way, the **ethereal spirit** occupies the middle place of the world, because it is composed of equal parts of water and light. Therefore, in its very center or midpoint, that **sphere of equality** is located, within which the **Sun** performs its motion. 

From this, it is clear that the Sun's composition consists of an equal part of water and light. Moving further upward from the Sun’s position, the proportion of light gradually increases over that of matter; conversely, as one moves downward, the proportion of matter gradually exceeds that of light. 

It is therefore evident that just as pure and absolute **Form** remains with God Himself—free from all created things and matter—so on the Earth, which is opposite to His throne and furthest from it, there is pure materiality and "corpulence" <note>original: "corpulentia"; referring to physical density, weight, and the lack of spiritual transparency.</note>. The Earth is free from all light, except for that which reaches it by chance <note>original: "contingentèr"; meaning light that falls upon it from the outside rather than being part of its internal essence.</note>. Consequently, that **sphere of equality** inhabited by the Sun occupies the middle ground between these two extremes. It receives a **spiritual harmony** from the divine power and, by the will of God, transmits that influence down to the Earth. Finally, the **Elemental Spirit**—which is the denser part of the "water"—consists of three parts of matter and only one part of form,

<summary>This page describes a mathematical cosmology where the universe is organized by the ratio of Light (Form) to Water (Matter), placing the Sun in a perfect "sphere of equality" between the spiritual heavens and the material Earth.</summary>

Therefore, the things contained in this region, being forged from its four degrees <note>likely referring to the four elements: earth, water, air, and fire.</note>, are subject to change and decay, because their appetite can never be fully satisfied due to the abundance of matter and the scarcity of form <note>In this philosophical system, "Form" is the active, divine spark, while "Matter" is the passive, heavy substance. Matter is said to "hunger" for Form to give it purpose.</note>. 

From this we can observe that, just as heat is an essential and active quality of light itself—stirred up and arising from its motion and vitality—so also coldness, its opposite, embraces opacity, darkness, and materiality. Thus it is certain that, just as the former quality is recognized as the effect of the actions of light, so also the latter [coldness] was drawn out from the bowels of *hyle* <term>hyle: a Greek term used by ancient philosophers to describe primordial, unformed matter</term> after the appearance of light, through the stirring of heat. From the intermediate actions of these two, humidity is generated, the absence of which is nothing other than dryness. And in this way, we have briefly declared the original causes both of light and of the waters or spirits <note>original: "spirituum"; here referring to subtle, vaporous substances that bridge the gap between physical matter and divine light.</note> found in this world.

We have discussed the theory of light and universal spirit in the three preceding chapters briefly, but clearly enough. Now, however, we shall say a few words regarding their practice and secret operations, through which we may reveal to the world that wonders—at least in relation to us and our human senses—can be performed through the secret benefit of clear and luminous nature, without any preceding deceptions of the Devil. To accomplish this, it is necessary that we inspect—with a keen, almost eagle-like gaze—the hidden disposition of the spiritual medium itself and the admirable power of clarity that illuminates that medium. ### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART III.

...[be] received. Therefore, this is that **philosophical sea** <note>original: *mare philosophicum*. A metaphorical term for the ether or the universal atmosphere that permeates all things.</note> in which **Angels**, **Souls**, and the **species** <term>species (here referring to the 'essences' or formal archetypes)</term> of things, both natural and artificial, both visible and invisible, swim no differently than fish in the visible and dense sea. From these things, therefore, we can observe how widely the mysteries of this **universal medium** extend, and what admirable operations arise from its natural property; operations which, being beyond the reach of common thought, might seem arduous or even impossible to the ignorant.

When these matters are considered again and again with the sharpest insight, we will find that it is not impossible that—through the mediation of this universal medium and its inherent clarity—someone might communicate with an absent person over several hundred miles, both by conversation and even by letters, within the space of a few hours. However, since an experiment of this kind could be performed in many ways, perhaps even supplemented by **impious Magic** or **Necromancy**, we will briefly explain the proper logic of it here.

It has been confirmed, both by trustworthy histories and by arguments resting upon natural and supernatural philosophy, that the aforementioned proposition and intention of the **brothers** <note>This likely refers to the "Fratres" or Brethren of the Rosy Cross (Rosicrucians), whom the author is defending.</note> can be brought to its desired end and pinnacle in two ways: namely, either by the divine inspiration and illumination of God, or by the benefit of a purely natural art. # ON THE SECRETS OF NATURE. 169

->ficial<- means, we may know that greater things are accomplished, and these by merely vulgar and common ones. For it is well known that if someone in the countryside lies down on the ground in a free and wide field, and takes a wooden rod or long staff, and puts one end of it in his ear, and places the remaining part along the ground near him, that he—I say—will hear people talking far and wide; indeed, we can even hear the sound of a bombard <term>bombard: an early type of cannon or mortar</term> or war engine from nine or ten miles away by this method. This is indeed achieved by the immense repercussion <note>original: *repercussione*; referring to the reflection or echoing of sound waves</note>, reverberation, and multiplication of the air. Likewise we see that in thick water, or the sea, a very small stone being thrown in disturbs a great quantity of water. So we see, if the end of a single string drawn out to a great length is touched, that part touched will immediately move the entire string. Indeed, in the observation of this secret lies hidden the mysterious operation of the Magnetic Ointment <note>original: *unguenti Magnetici*; a reference to the "weapon salve" or "sympathetic ointment" believed to heal wounds by being applied to the weapon that caused them, operating via a supposed natural attraction or "magnetism" over a distance.</note>, which we shall pass over in silence here. ## CHAPTER VI.

### *Concerning the hidden and wonderful effects of secret Music.*

*Whoever desires to sing with the voice of the mouth or a musical instrument in such a way that he attracts not those stones of **Amphion** <note>In Greek mythology, Amphion's music was so powerful that stones moved on their own to build the walls of Thebes.</note>, but pearls and gems; nor the beasts of **Orpheus** <note>A legendary musician whose song could charm all living things, even stones and trees.</note>, but spirits; not **Pluto** from **Tartarus** <note>Pluto is the god of the underworld, and Tartarus is the deep abyss of torment in Greek mythology.</note>, but the powerful princes of the world: let him enter the **Fraternity**.*

If we grant any credit to the opinions of **Plato** and **Cardanus** <note>Gerolamo Cardano (1501–1576), an Italian polymath who wrote on the "harmony of the spheres."</note>, the finest harmony is generated by the **conglomeration of the spheres** <term>conglomeration of the spheres</term> <note>original: "sphærarum conglomeratione"; the ancient and medieval concept that the movement of celestial bodies creates a perfect, divine music.</note>, which cannot be perceived by our ears due to the great distance. Nevertheless, from its effects, not only the **symphonic consonances** of this music are recognized in these lower realms, but also its **dissonances**. For the former excite love, symphony, and a lovely longing in things, while the latter stir up hatred, discord, and a deadly contempt. For it is from the symphonic influence of the heavens that it arises that the **Magnet** <note>original: "Magnes"</note> attracts iron, **Amber** <note>original: "Electrum" and "Carabe"; both refer to fossilized resin which, when rubbed, creates static electricity to attract small objects like chaff.</note> attracts silver, hairs, and chaff, and the **magnetic ointment** <note>original: "unguentum magneticum"; a substance used in "sympathetic medicine," believed to heal wounds by being applied to the weapon that caused them, acting through a natural attraction or "magnetism."</note> [treats] nature...

APOLOGETIC TREATISE PART III.

...look back upon the nature of the wounded with wondrous love, and attract them to themselves. Truly, their dissonant influence produces an **antipathy** <term>antipathy</term> <note>original: "antipathiam"; a natural or deep-seated feeling of dislike or aversion; the opposite of sympathy.</note> and discord among things, by which the Lamb is set against the Wolf, Dormice against the cat, the song of the Cock against the lion, the sight of the **Basilisk** <note>A legendary reptile reputed to be king of serpents and said to have the power to cause death with a single glance.</note> or the **Catoblephas** <term>Catoblepas</term> <note>original: "Catoblephæ"; a mythical Ethiopian creature often described as a bull-like animal whose heavy head always points down and whose gaze or breath can be lethal.</note> against man, and infinite other things against others. For by the concord of divine Music, like things draw themselves together for the sake of conservation; yet by the discord of the same, unlike and contrary things mutually repel and flee from one another for that same end.

For there are certain stars which terminate their more perfect consonances in discordant places (just as we shall clearly and by infallible demonstration declare elsewhere in our **worldly harmony** <note>original: "harmonia nostra mundana"; likely a reference to Fludd's major work, *Utriusque Cosmi Historia*, where he explores the musical proportions of the universe.</note>). From this it happens that **Saturn** (since it is cold and dry in its nature, yet is in the element of fire, which is hot) ends its **Diapason** <term>Diapason</term> <note>The musical interval of an octave; in Pythagorean thought, this represents the most perfect consonance.</note>; and **Mars** (since it is hot and dry, yet is in the element of water, which is cold and moist) terminates its Diapason. Thus, by accident, they are malicious and unfortunate, even though in themselves they are as good as the rest of the Planets, the Sun excepted. 

To the contrary, however, **Jupiter**, which is hot and moist, terminates its Diapason in the element of its own nature—namely, in the air—with which **Venus** (who is temperate in **complexion** <term>complexion</term> <note>original: "complexione"; in medieval medicine and alchemy, the "complexion" refers to the specific balance of the four humors or qualities—hot, cold, moist, dry—within a person or planet.</note>) accords in a **Diapente** <term>Diapente</term> <note>A musical interval of a perfect fifth.</note>. Whence it happens that sublunary creatures, receiving the complexions of these good planets, [join] one another with love and...

...considered certain, and it will be probable that, since the unison of celestial Harmony exists in sound and voice, the same consonance can also happen by virtue of a medium or aerial spirit, by which the bodies of pearls and Gems might be moved: and this is demonstrated by the following experiment. For if we place two **Citharas** <term>Cithara</term> <note>original: "Citharas"; an ancient stringed instrument, here referring to a lute or similar Renaissance instrument used in acoustic experiments.</note> upon the same table, and place a straw upon a string of one, if the string of the other—sounding in unison to that string carrying the straw—is struck, the string holding the straw will immediately vibrate and move, and will cast off its straw with a sudden motion: from which we can gather the wonderful relationship of one creature to another by virtue of this harmony. 

Let us diligently examine the fables of the poets, and we shall find immense secrets beneath them. Why did **Pan** (by whom all of universal nature is signified) compose a pipe from seven **syringes** or reeds <term>syringes</term> <note>original: "syringibus"; the individual pipes of a Pan-flute, named after the nymph Syrinx.</note>, through which he produced sweet harmony, if not because the intellectual spirit, which moves the heavens, creates a corresponding music in these lower realms? For by the composition of the seven pipes is signified the gathering of the seven orbits of the planets and their wonderful harmony in heaven and on earth—that is, wherever that universal nature extends itself. Similarly, let us seek the reason why **Mercury** gave the **Cithara** to **Apollo**, just as he, in turn, gave the **Caduceus** <term>Caduceus</term> <note>original: "Caduceum"; the herald's staff entwined with two snakes, representing the reconciliation of opposites and alchemical balance.</note> rod to Mercury.

By these symphonic consonances, they secretly modulate the hidden **music of lives** <note>original: "musicam vitarum arcanam"</note>. The audible sounds of this music lie hidden within creatures no differently than fire lies within wood, or in flint, or in the turpentine tree; the hidden fires of these things are rendered visible, and are brought **from potentiality into act** <term>from potentiality into act</term> <note>original: "ad actum de potentia"; a Scholastic philosophical concept where a latent capacity becomes a realized reality.</note>, either by the strike of iron or by some other actual fire similar to itself. 

Therefore, for the one to whom it is given to know the true **Phoebean tones** <note>Tones associated with Phoebus Apollo, the sun god.</note>, and to bring them forth into a sensible act, it will not be an impossible thing to use their harmony to attract and allure all things originally forged by him. ...it contains things truly admirable and clearly stupendous within itself. It must therefore be understood that the great <note>Likely referring to the Macrocosm, or the great world.</note> contains within itself infinite smaller things, which are locked away by seals or characters <term>characters</term> <note>original: "characteribus"; mystical or symbolic marks believed to encode the inner essence or "signature" of a thing.</note> that constitute the visible form of a creature. For the external form of a creature is like the bark, or the covering of a specific "little book of Nature" <note>original: "Naturæ libelli"</note>; unless this is wisely removed and the hidden things are revealed, it will be impossible to draw out its treasure, or to read and understand the secret characters of that same nature.

For the shapes of created things are most aptly compared to chests which, once closed, hide even from our eyes the gems enclosed within them; nor will it be possible to gaze upon them further unless those chests are opened again. I have, therefore, taken this sort of little chest of Nature—or this covering of a little book—which is of the smallest capacity and yet of the greatest renown among men, of infinite necessity and immense divinity (indeed, esteemed by some as a "natural God" <note>original: "pro Deo naturali"</note>), and of such antiquity or duration that it even existed as the sweet and nourishing companion of **Adam** while he lived his life in Paradise.

# APOLOGETIC TREATISE PART III.

...what **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure representing a synthesis of the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth, regarded as the author of the Hermetic Corpus.</note> intended by these words: <note>original: "Cum omnia indistincta fuissent, levia postmodum in excelsam regionem provolârint, & gravia sub arena humida resederunt. Distinctis libratisque rebus qua igneo spiritu vehebantur, emicuit cælum septem in circulos."</note> "When all things had been indistinct, the light ones soon flew up into the high region, and the heavy ones settled beneath the damp sand. Once things were separated and balanced, being carried by a fiery spirit, the sky flashed forth into seven circles." Or what **Hippocrates** <note>An ancient Greek physician often called the "Father of Medicine."</note> meant by a discourse of this kind: <note>original: "Calidum immortale, cum omnia essent conturbata in supremam peripheriam discessit, quam veteres æthera dixerunt."</note> "The immortal heat, when all things were in confusion, departed into the highest periphery, which the ancients called the **ether** <term>ether</term> <note>original: "æthera"; the "fifth element" or the pure, fiery substance of the highest heavens.</note>."

### 3. It is clearly demonstrated that, contrary to the views of certain philosophers, light is the most subtle substance of all things and not an accident, in which the life and motion of all created things consist.
<note>In Aristotelian philosophy, an "accident" is a property that does not exist by itself (like the color of a rose). The author argues here that **light** (lux) is a substantial, physical building block of the universe, not just a property.</note>

### 4. What Nature itself is, and what is the ligament and bond through which the four clashing elements are connected together in harmonious peace, both in the worldly machine and in the composition of any creature?
Hence **Pythagoras** <note>A Greek philosopher who taught that the universe is governed by mathematical ratios and musical harmony.</note> called the soul of things a **harmony** <term>harmony</term> <note>original: "harmoniam"; the principle of proportion and agreement that prevents the elements from destroying one another.</note>, or a melody and concord.

### 5. How the slightest defect of this same bond is a sign of a deficiency of the ethereal spirit in a composite body?
For if the **bond** <term>bond</term> <note>original: "ligamentum"; the metaphysical "glue" that holds the diverse elements of the physical world together in a stable form.</note> fails in any part of a complex body, the life and movement of that part will be instantly taken away; if it fails in the whole, it will bring death to the entire thing. But if this **celestial balsam** <term>celestial balsam</term> <note>original: "balsamum cœleste"; a life-giving, healing substance thought to be derived from the heavens or the stars.</note> is very weak in a living creature—whether in part or in whole—where its quantity is nonetheless sufficient, the animal or plant will fall ill in whole or in part, yet in such a way that it can easily be restored to its former health. However, if it is weak due to a deficiency and smallness of its quantity, the body wastes away and, without that great **medicine of Nature** <note>A fundamental curative power inherent in the universe, often sought by alchemists and physicians of this period to restore the "vital spark."</note> which is of its own nature, it cannot be restored and refreshed. For where this bond is not strong enough, it happens that the elementary harmony is immediately corrupted, and is stained and disturbed by discords.

### 6. What is the invisible fire of Zoroaster and Heraclitus, from which they claimed all things were born?
<note>Zoroaster (the Persian prophet) and Heraclitus (the Greek philosopher) both emphasized the role of fire as the primary element or creative force of the universe.</note>

17. *That the vegetative soul* <note>In traditional philosophy, the "vegetative soul" is the lowest level of the soul responsible for growth and reproduction, common to plants and animals alike.</note> *always accompanies the vital soul.*

18. *Why the Quintessence* <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: "Quinta essentia"; the "fifth essence," a pure, incorruptible substance beyond the four earthly elements.</note> *is called animal, vegetable, and mineral by the Philosophers.*

19. *How any animal is nourished invisibly by this quintessence in a two-fold way: Namely, either insofar as it is hidden in the air—from which it happens that the air drawn from the lungs through breathing to the heart, by every inhalation, carries with it a hidden portion of the quintessence, which is dispersed and dispensed through the arteries to nourish the vital spirits* <term>vital spirits</term> <note>original: "spiritus vitales"; in early modern medicine, these were subtle substances produced in the heart that carried life and heat throughout the body.</note> *contained and gathered in them. Or insofar as it is collected and condensed secretly in the composite body of the food itself, or nourishment, so that this gathered heavenly light makes a thick, heavenly, visible fire, which nourishes the nutritive and natural faculties; a more spiritual part of this fire also penetrates and is transported through diapedesis* <term>diapedesis</term> <note>original: "diapedesin"; here referring to the oozing or passage of subtle substances through the walls of vessels or tissues.</note> *to the chambers of the heart and is dispersed everywhere through the arteries. Its thicker part, indeed, is mixed with the subtle part of the elements in the veins with the blood, for the complete constitution of the same, so that the more solid parts may be nourished and preserved by it. And it is necessary that some of this heavenly fire be united with the thick part of the elements, for the preservation of the natural bond of the entire composition; although the greater portion of that supplementary bond is derived from the orifices of the arteries. I have been (I say) an eyewitness that neither animal, nor vegetable, nor mineral could have lived, endured, or existed for a single minute of an hour without this bright fire.*

20. *That the heavenly Soul with its vehicles gives motion, action, and every function to any animal and plant in no other way...*

<meta>catchword: function</meta>

<summary>This page outlines Robert Fludd's theory on the "heavenly fire" or quintessence that sustains life, explaining how it is absorbed through both breathing and digestion to nourish the vital spirits. He emphasizes that no living or mineral thing can exist even for a moment without this spiritual light.</summary>
<keywords>soul, quintessence, vital spirits, heavenly fire, nutrition, respiration, Robert Fludd, alchemy, physiology</keywords>

### ON THE SECRETS OF NATURE.

...[performing the same] function <note>original: "functionem"</note> which the Sun performs for each element and the entire world, according to the opinion of Plato and Plotinus.

21. How the *Vital and Vegetative Soul*, by means of two vehicles—namely, the ethereal and aerial spirit—is introduced into the opaque body. And that the ethereal spirit is the radical moisture <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: "humidum radicale"; in medieval and Renaissance medicine, this was the essential fluid of life, often compared to the oil in a lamp that keeps the fire of life burning.</note> of a thing, and acts as the fuel for the vital fire, just as that celestial light is the innate heat of the creature, or the active force for life within that moisture.

22. Concerning the misfortunes of the *Bright Soul* <note>original: "Animae lucidae"</note> in the *opaque body* and the reason for them.

23. A demonstration of the causes of "hot" diseases, and similarly on the difference between *burnt Cholera* <term>burnt Cholera</term> <note>original: "Cholera adusta"; this refers to yellow bile that has been "burnt" by excessive heat, a condition believed to cause madness or violent illness.</note> and other types: and how the evil spirit, or the Devil, mingles with these bilious and burnt humors, according to these words of *Artephius* <note>Artephius was a famous medieval alchemist, often cited as having lived for over a thousand years through his mastery of the philosopher's stone.</note>:
> *Perhaps there are certain infirmities with which the Devil joins himself, alongside the humor that is causing the ailment and disturbing one's health, and this happens most especially in diseases of cholera.* 
The reason is that the Devil’s body is composed of air and fire; his visible part is air, but his hidden part is fire, which is why he cannot be seen by human eyes. Since his hidden part is of the nature of fire—the same nature as Cholera—he therefore easily joins himself with *burnt Cholera*, and so on.

24. The cause of melancholy proceeding from *burnt Cholera*. Why men infected by such a humor are sad and fearful. Why they flee the light, love the darkness, and why their reason is corrupted and stained by the most wretched imaginings.

25. That before the dissolution and corruption of a natural thing is completed, it is necessary that the quintessence first depart: and that at the moment of its departure, the life-giving way in which the elements of the species are structured is dissolved.

26. How, when an animal dies, its ethereal spirit does not depart without its *bright soul*, for which it serves as a vehicle both in its descent into the body and in its ascent into heaven.

27. The reason for the generation of the serpent Python from the mud of the earth, and that the *Sensitive Soul* <note>The "sensitive soul" is the level of the soul responsible for perception and movement, shared by animals and humans.</note> follows the heat of the Sun, since

<meta>catchword: soul</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the relationship between the "Bright Soul" and the physical body, detailing how biological life is sustained by radical moisture and vital heat. It also provides a physiological and demonic explanation for illnesses like melancholy and cholera, linking them to the "burning" of bodily humors.</summary>

<keywords>vital soul, radical moisture, burnt cholera, Artephius, melancholy, quintessence, sensitive soul, bright soul, demonic influence</keywords>

# APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART III.

<meta>The catchword "Hæc" (These) suggests the next page begins a list or a new sentence starting with that word.</meta>

*the <term>vegetative soul<note>original: "anima vegetativa"; the most basic level of life responsible for growth and reproduction in plants and animals</note> proceeds from it, as was apparent in the generation of the <term>Python<note>A reference to the mythological serpent born from the earth's mud after the great flood; often used by natural philosophers to discuss spontaneous generation</note>.*

And infinite other secrets of this kind concerning the soul and the body of the living being, we are able to collect from this little book of Nature; and with proper contemplation, even with our material eyes, we can as it were read and behold them. It would be tedious to express all of these things in this place. But now, by looking upward with the eyes of the intellect above the Solar body, we perceive the reasoning behind secrets of this kind, namely:

## Concerning the Gods, and the Rational Soul.

<margin>28.</margin> Why (*according to the opinions of Democritus, Orpheus, and many <term>Pythagoreans<note>Followers of Pythagoras who believed in the transmigration of souls and a mathematical universe</note>*) a certain God or Soul presides over every natural thing; and why they said that all things are full of Gods, all of whom they referred back to the one <term>Jupiter<note>original: "Iovem"; here used as a philosophical name for the supreme deity or the unifying principle of the universe</note>*. And why they referred all Souls back to the one <term>World-Soul<note>original: "mundi Animam"; the concept that the entire universe is a single living organism with a soul</note>*. Likewise, how the <term>Papists<note>original: "Papistæ"; a historical term for Roman Catholics</note> in the Mass* almost approach the opinions of these philosophers. Why the <term>Platonists<note>original: "Platonici"; followers of Plato's philosophy</note>* said, *the Soul is in the Spirit and the spirit is in the body:* And <term>Hermes Trismegistus<note>original: "Mercurius Trismegistus"; the legendary founder of Hermeticism and alchemy</note>*, elevated to an even higher degree: *The <term>Mind<note>original: "Mens"; the highest intellectual faculty</note> is in the <term>reason<note>original: "ratione"; the logical faculty</note>, the Reason is in the Soul, the Soul is in the spirit, and the spirit is in the body.* And why they maintained that the rational part is the receptacle of the Mind or intellectual ray in no other way than the ethereal spirit is the vehicle of the vital Soul. For this reason, they maintain that the Mind and intellect are carried in their own vehicle or rational chariot, which is the <term>Empyrean spirit<note>original: "spiritu Empyreo"; the highest, most refined spirit associated with the heavens of pure fire</note>*, and this Empyrean or rational spirit is carried in the luminous soul of life, and that soul in the ethereal spirit, and that spirit in the air, and the air (along with all the others) in the potential body or human seed.

<summary>This page explores the hierarchy of the soul and its connection to the divine, drawing on ancient Greek, Hermetic, and Neoplatonic traditions to explain how the mind descends through various "vehicles" into the human body. The author links these philosophical views on the World-Soul and presiding deities to the liturgical practices of the Catholic Mass.</summary>

<keywords>vegetative soul, Python, World-Soul, Jupiter, Hermes Trismegistus, Empyrean spirit, Neoplatonism, mind-body hierarchy, vital soul, rational chariot</keywords>

<meta>The previous page concluded with a discussion on the opinions of the Platonists regarding the World-Soul and the rational soul. This page continues the defense of the author's observations and their alignment with the Rosicrucian manifestos.</meta>

# ON THE SECRETS OF NATURE.

All these things truly experienced by us (Kind Reader), we have resolved to commit to writing and place before your eyes, all the more so that you may place more certain faith in those propositions published to the world by the <term>Fraternity of the R.C.<note>original: "Fraternitate R. C."; refers to the Rosicrucians, a mysterious brotherhood of philosophers and physicians whose manifestos in the early 17th century called for a universal reformation of science and religion.</note>. They themselves, with an open gaze, beheld Nature naked and revealed, and did not hesitate to open the volume of universal Nature and to read and understand her <term>secret characters<note>original: "characteres ejusdem arcanos"; this refers to the "Signatures of Things," the belief that God marked all created objects with hidden signs or symbols that reveal their inner properties and purposes.</note>. We, however, have leafed through only a single small book—or rather the smallest part—of that great volume, in a crude and confused manner.

But you, perhaps with a darkened mind and stubborn incredulity, will reject those assertions of the Brothers and these of ours, judging them to be more absurd than lies or more mythical than fables, despite being truly presented. For many wonders, and things almost unprecedented, are accustomed to happen to me, to you, and to all men, which nevertheless lose their credibility when related to the ignorant. For frequently, due to very corrupt opinions, those things are thought to be fables and lies which seem either new to the hearing or crude to the sight, or certainly high above the grasp of thought; but if we investigate them a little more accurately, we shall find them not only evident to the discovery, but even easy in the doing. Therefore, I will swear to you by God, the seeing Sun of the world, that I have recorded true findings; and I say this now with such sincerity so that you [may proceed] with greater confidence in these our experiences...

<sig>N</sig>
<meta>catchword: mind [original: "mentis"]</meta>

<summary>The author defends the validity of his findings and the claims of the Rosicrucian Fraternity, arguing that many truths are dismissed as fables simply because they surpass the common man's understanding or experience.</summary>
<keywords>Rosicrucianism, Book of Nature, secret characters, skepticism, scientific observation, signatures of nature</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>This page concludes the third part of the "Apologetic Treatise." It follows the author's argument that the Rosicrucian claims—often dismissed by the public as fables—are rooted in a direct, "anatomical" observation of nature's hidden truths. The author transitions from defending the Brothers to expressing personal gratitude for their enlightenment.</meta>

### APOLOGETIC TREATISE, PART III.

...shining with the power of the mind, do not reject with dull ears and a hardened heart these greater and more illustrious discourses, and these more admirable effects, which have been tossed about by the **Brothers** <note>original: "Fratribus"; specifically the Brethren of the Rosy Cross.</note> through the mouths of the people. For these truths have now undoubtedly emerged—carried on the wings of truth and conscience—above the lies of the envious, the slanders of the wicked, and the disbelief of the common folk.

I frankly confess, therefore, that these **Brothers**, who are worthy of all admiration, have soothed my mind (which is most greedy for knowledge) with the peaceful melody of their wisdom. They have done so all the more because I have seen with these very eyes, more clearly, the secrets hiding in the lap of nature, and those things recondite beneath the **bark** <note>original: "cortice"; a common metaphor in hermetic literature suggesting that the physical exterior of an object hides its true spiritual or medicinal essence.</note> or exterior form of created things; I have observed these sedulously through a diligent <term>anatomization of species<note>original: "specierum anatomizatione"; in this context, "anatomy" refers not just to dissection, but to the deep, systematic analysis of the internal properties and signatures of different types of natural things.</note>.

Wherefore I confidently believe them and record my grateful thanks, because by the sweetness of true wisdom and a divine whispering, they have called me away from the potions, illusions, and deceptions of the crude and erroneous **Philosophy** of the world.

Oh, if only I had served my first apprenticeships of childhood in the **Cloister** of this wisdom! For I have finally discovered that, through the lack of it, worldly men continually drink the cup of mourning and unhappiness, and even have ignorance as a companion to their calamity.

Behold, we have completed the duty of our promise, for we have finally woven together the rest of our **Apology**, which was begun at the <term>Spring Fair<note>original: "Nundinis vernalibus"; likely a reference to the Frankfurt Book Fair, the primary event for the distribution of intellectual and scientific works in the 17th century, where the first part of this defense was likely released.</note>.

->**Farewell.**<-

<summary>In this concluding passage, the author defends the Rosicrucian "Brothers" against public skepticism, asserting that their wisdom is based on a deep, "anatomical" understanding of nature's secrets. He expresses a personal spiritual conversion, thanking the Brotherhood for rescuing him from the "illusions" of traditional worldly philosophy.</summary>

<keywords>Rosicrucians, secrets of nature, anatomy of species, Frankfurt Book Fair, true wisdom, philosophical deception, apology, spiritual enlightenment</keywords>

# AUTHOR’S EPILOGUE
## TO THE BROTHERS
### OF THE
# ROSY CROSS.

O you most beloved Brothers, who profess to rebuild in yourselves—through a mutual striving—the spiritual forms of men and more blessed fortunes, which had been turned into more wretched images by the fall and misfortune of Adam <note>In Rosicrucian thought, the "Fall of Adam" represents not just a moral failing, but a loss of original spiritual perception and "divine anatomy," which the Brothers sought to restore through alchemy and prayer.</note>: greetings, I say, in that very one whom you worship purely and sincerely, Jesus Christ, the Savior of the world.

See here, I beg for pardon if in this little work of mine, I—an unrefined philosopher <note>original: "Philosophus"; in the 17th century, this term often referred to a "natural philosopher" or "alchemist" seeking the hidden laws of God's creation.</note> and unworthy proclaimer of your renown—have caused offense through any blatant error or halting knowledge. Who I am, receive in these few words: one who would most gladly and eagerly place himself last among the companions of your order, so that I might soothe the thirsty ears of mankind with a more worthy and certain whisper of your praise. By nation, rank, residence, and name as stated above, I am of noble enough birth; but to me, my wife is the desire for wisdom; my children are the fruits arising from it; my body is a prison; and the pleasures of the world are vain and fatal to the soul. I thirst for...

<sig>N 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: myself</meta>

<summary>This page serves as the formal epilogue to the treatise, where the author addresses the Rosicrucian Brotherhood with deep humility and offers a spiritual autobiography. He characterizes his life's work as an attempt to recover the divine state lost by Adam and expresses a desire to join the lowest ranks of the order.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the author's personal epilogue. Following his declaration on the previous page that his body is a prison and his only desire is wisdom, he now reflects on his extensive travels and his disillusionment with the material world.</meta>

<header>TREATISE APPENDIX PART III. ON THE SECRETS OF NATURE.</header>

...myself as a mirror, through which I might contemplate what I am. I have surveyed with my eyes and mind almost all the provinces of Europe: the churning and deep straits, the steep mountains, the slippery valleys, the rough villages, the unrefined towns, the proud cities—the ambitious, the greedy, the unfaithful, the ignorant, the lazy, and the treacherous—and emerging from almost all the unhappy conditions of men, I have found no one reaching the peak of happiness <term>happiness: original "felicitas"; here meaning spiritual blessedness or the "Great Work" of the soul</term>, or correctly knowing themselves, according to that saying of Saint John, Chapter 1: *The life was the light, and this light was in the darkness, and the darkness did not comprehend it.* <note>original: "Vita erat lumen, & hoc lumen erat in tenebris & tenebra non comprehenderunt illud"; John 1:4-5. The author uses this to suggest that divine truth is present but ignored by the worldly.</note> Therefore, I have discovered that nothing except vanity triumphs almost everywhere throughout the world. Vanity (I say) of vanities <note>original: "Vanitas vanitatum"; a reference to Ecclesiastes 1:2, emphasizing the emptiness of earthly pursuits compared to spiritual truth.</note> is found everywhere, and all things are as if misery and vanity. And so now I depart. Farewell (most sweet Brothers) in that very one whom you sincerely worship. Farewell (I say) and again farewell; and (by your faith, and by the ignorance of true and pure Philosophy in this age, I pray and beseech you) favor me, be present with me: and of me
